&=O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}}\left(\Delta_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}(\mathcal{X}))\right)=O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))\qquad\mbox{(by \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{clm:localPotenDecomps})}\penalty\ .\end{split}

(34)

Next, we bound
w(F^{(2)}_{i})
. Let
(u,v)
be an edge added to
H_{i}
in Step 2 and let
(\varphi_{C_{u}},\varphi_{C_{v}})
be the corresponding edge of
(u,v)
. Since
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}_{i}\not=\mathcal{V}_{i}
, at least one of the endpoints of
(\varphi_{C_{u}},\varphi_{C_{v}})
, w.l.o.g.
\varphi_{C_{u}}
, is in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{+}}_{i}
by
7.5
. Recall by Item (1) of
Lemma

7.3
that all nodes in
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathsf{low}^{+}}
have low degree, i.e., incident to
O(1/\epsilon)
edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
. Thus,
|F^{(2)}_{i}|=O(\frac{1}{\epsilon})|\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathsf{low}^{+}}|
. We have:

\begin{split}w(F^{(2)}_{i})&=O(\frac{1}{\epsilon})|\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathsf{low}^{+}}|L_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\tiny\mbox{Eq.\penalty\ }(\ref{eq:mvilowhigh})}}}{{=}}O(\frac{1}{\epsilon})\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{low}^{+}}}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i}\penalty\ =\penalty\ O(\frac{1}{\epsilon})\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{high}}\cup\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{low}^{+}}}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\tiny{\mbox{Eq.\penalty\ }}(\ref{eq:averagePotential})}}}{{=}}O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}})\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{high}}\cup\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{low}^{+}}}\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}})(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))\penalty\ .\end{split}

(35)

By property (1) of
\mathsf{SSA}
, the number of edges added to
H_{i}
in Step 3 is at most
\chi|\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}|
. Thus:

\begin{split}w(F^{(3)}_{i})&\penalty\ \leq\penalty\ \chi|\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathsf{high}}|L_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\tiny\mbox{Eq.\penalty\ }(\ref{eq:mvilowhigh})}}}{{\leq}}\chi\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{high}}}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i}\penalty\ \leq\penalty\ \chi\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{high}}\cup\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{low}^{+}}}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\tiny{\mbox{Eq.\penalty\ }}(\ref{eq:averagePotential})}}}{{=}}O(\chi\epsilon^{-2})\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{high}}\cup\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{low}^{+}}}\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=O(\chi\epsilon^{-2})(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))\penalty\ .\end{split}

(36)

By
Equations

34
,
35
and
36
, we conclude that:

\begin{split}w(H_{i})&=O(\chi\epsilon^{-2}+\epsilon^{-3})(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))\leq\lambda(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))\end{split}

(37)

for some
\lambda=O(\chi\epsilon^{-2}+\epsilon^{-3})
.

It remains to consider the degenerate case where
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}=\mathcal{V}_{i}
. Even if we add every single edge that corresponds to an edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
to
H_{i}
, Item (3) in
Lemma

7.3
implies that the number of such edges is at most
O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})
. Thus, we have:

w(H_{i})=O(\frac{L_{i}}{\epsilon^{2}})\leq\lambda\cdot(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))+O(\frac{L_{i}}{\epsilon^{2}})

(38)

where in the last equation, we use the fact that:

\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\footnotesize{\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{clm:localPotenDecomps}}}}}{{=}}\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}}(\Delta_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})+\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}(\mathcal{X}))=\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}}\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})\geq 0\penalty\

by Item (3) of
Lemma

7.3
. Thus, the claim follows from
Equations

38
and
37
.

\Box

Proof:
[Proof of
Lemma

7.7
] The running time follows from
7.9
. By
7.11
, the stretch is
t(1+\max\{s_{\mathsf{SSA}}(2g)+4g,10g\}\epsilon)
. By
7.12
, we have

\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}a_{i}=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}(\lambda\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}+O(L_{i}/\epsilon^{2}))
. Observe by the definition that the sets of corresponding edges of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}
and
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{j}
are disjoint for any
i\not=j\geq 1
. Thus,
\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}\leq w(\mathrm{MST})
. Observe that:

\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}O(\frac{L_{i}}{\epsilon^{2}})\penalty\ =\penalty\ O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})\sum_{i=1}^{i_{\max}}\frac{L_{i_{\max}}}{\epsilon^{i_{\max}-i}}\penalty\ =\penalty\ O(\frac{L_{i_{\max}}}{\epsilon^{2}(1-\epsilon)})\penalty\ =\penalty\ O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})w(\mathrm{MST})\penalty\ ;

here
i_{\max}
is the maximum level. The last equation is due to that
\epsilon\leq 1/2
and every edge has weight at most
w(\mathrm{MST})
(by the removal step in the construction of
\tilde{G}
). Thus,
A=\lambda+O(\epsilon^{-2})=O(\chi\epsilon^{-2}+\epsilon^{-3})+O(\epsilon^{-2})=O(\chi\epsilon^{-2}+\epsilon^{-3})
as claimed.

\Box

We are now ready to prove Item (1) of
Theorem

1.10
.

Proof of Item (1) of
Theorem

1.10
.

By
Lemma

7.3
and
Lemma

7.6
, level-
(i+1)
clusters can be constructed in time
O((|\mathcal{V}_{i}|+|\mathcal{E}_{i}|)\epsilon^{-1}+|\mathcal{V}_{i}|\alpha(m,n))=O((|\mathcal{C}_{i}|+|E^{\sigma}_{i}|)(\alpha(m,n)+\epsilon^{-1})
when
\epsilon\ll 1
. By
Lemma

7.7
,
H_{i}
can be constructed in time
O((|\mathcal{V}_{i}|+|\mathcal{E}_{i}|)\tau(m,n))=O((|\mathcal{C}_{i}|+|E^{\sigma}_{i}|)\tau(m,n)
.

We can construct a minimum spanning tree in time
T_{\mathrm{MST}}=O((n+m)\alpha(m,n))
by using Chazelle’s algorithm
[
16
]
. Thus, by
Lemma

6.8
, the construction time of the light spanner is

O(m\epsilon^{-1}(\tau(m,n)+\alpha(m,n)+\epsilon^{-1})\log(1/\epsilon)+T_{\mathrm{MST}})=O(m\epsilon^{-1}(\tau(m,n)+\alpha(m,n)+\epsilon^{-1})\log(1/\epsilon)\penalty\ .

By
Lemma

7.7
and
Lemma

6.8
, the lightness of the spanner is

O(\frac{\lambda+A+1}{\epsilon}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon}+\frac{1}{\epsilon})=O((\chi\epsilon^{-3}+\epsilon^{-4})\log(1/\epsilon)).

Note that we set
\psi=\epsilon
in this case. Since
g=31
, by
Lemma

7.7
and
Lemma

6.8
, the stretch of the spanner is

t(1+\max\{s_{\mathsf{SSA}}(2g)+4g,10g\}\epsilon)\leq t(1+(s_{\mathsf{SSA}}O(1))+O(1))\epsilon)\penalty\ .

This completes the proof of the theorem.

\Box

8
Clustering: Proof of
Lemma

7.3

In this section, we construct the set of subgraph
\mathbb{X}
of the cluster graph
\mathcal{G}_{i}=(\mathcal{V}_{i},\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}\cup\mathcal{E}_{i},\omega)
as claimed in
Lemma

7.3
. See
Table

1
for a summary of notation we introduced in
Section

6
. Our construction builds upon the construction of Borradaile, Le and Wulff-Nilsen (BLW)
[
10
]
. However, unlike their construction, which is inefficient, our main focus here is on having a
linear-time
construction. Using the augmented diameter, we could bound the size of subgraphs (specifically in the construction of Step 4) arising during the course of our algorithm, and compute the augmented diameters of clusters efficiently. We note that in Borradaile, Le and Wulff-Nilsen
[
10
]
, the efficiency of the construction is not relevant since they use the cluster hierarchy to
analyze the greedy algorithm
, not in the construction of the spanner.

Our construction has five main steps (Steps 1-5). In Step 1, we group all vertices of
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}
and their neighbors into subgraphs of
\mathbb{X}
; see
Lemma

8.1
. In Step 2, we deal with
branching nodes
of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
; see
Lemma

8.2
. In Step 3, we augment existing subgraphs formed in Steps 1 and 2, to guarantee a special structure of the ungrouped nodes. In Step 4, we group subpaths of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
connected by an edge
\mathbf{e}
in
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
into clusters; see
Lemma

8.4
. Finally, in Step 5, we deal with the remaining nodes of
\mathcal{V}_{i}
.

Recall that
g
is a constant defined in
property (P3)
(by
Lemma

7.3
,
g=31
), and that
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
is a spanning tree of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
. We refer readers to
Table

1
for a summary of the notation.

Lemma 8.1

(Step 1)
.

Let
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}=\{\varphi_{C}\in\mathcal{V}:\varphi_{C}\mbox{ is incident to at least $\frac{2g}{\epsilon}$ edges in }\mathcal{E}_{i}\}
. Let
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathsf{high}+}
be obtained from
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}
by adding all neighbors that are connected to nodes in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}
via edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
. We can construct in
O(|\mathcal{V}_{i}|+|\mathcal{E}_{i}|)
time a collection of node-disjoint subgraphs
\mathbb{X}_{1}
of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
such that:

(1)

Each subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}
is a tree.

(2)

\cup_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}}\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}+}_{i}
.

(3)

L_{i}\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq 13L_{i}
, assuming that
\epsilon\leq 1/g
.

(4)

|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\geq\frac{2g}{\epsilon}
.

Proof:
Let
\mathcal{J}=(\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathcal{E}_{i})
be the subgraph of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
with the same vertex set and with edge set
\mathcal{E}_{i}
. Let
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{J}}(\varphi)
be the set of neighbors of a node
\varphi
in
\mathcal{J}
, and
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{J}}[\varphi]=\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{J}}(\varphi)\cup\{\varphi\}
. We construct
\mathbb{X}_{1}
in three steps; initially,
\mathbb{X}_{1}=\emptyset
.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{I}
be a
maximal
set of nodes in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}
such that for any two nodes
\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\in\mathcal{I}
,
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{J}}[\varphi_{1}]\cap\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{J}}[\varphi_{2}]=\emptyset
. For each node
\varphi\in\mathcal{I}
, we form a subgraph
\mathcal{X}
that consists of
\varphi
, its neighbors
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{J}}[\varphi]
, and all incident edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
of
\varphi
. We then add
\mathcal{X}
to
\mathbb{X}_{1}
.

(2)

We iterate over all nodes of
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{I}
that are not grouped yet to any subgraph. For each such node
\varphi\in\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{I}
, there must be a neighbor
\varphi^{\prime}
that is already grouped to a subgraph, say
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}
; if there are multiple such neighbors, we pick one of them arbitrarily. We add
\varphi
and the edge
(\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})
to
\mathcal{X}
. Observe that every node in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}
is grouped to some subgraph at the end of this step.

(3)

For each node
\varphi
in
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathsf{high}+}
that has not grouped to a subgraph in steps (1) and (2), there must be at least one neighbor, say
\varphi^{\prime}
, of
\varphi
that is grouped in step (1) or step (2) to a subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}
; if there are multiple such nodes, we pick one of them arbitrarily. We then add
\varphi
and the edge
(\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})
to
\mathcal{X}
.

This completes the construction of
\mathbb{X}_{1}
. We now show that subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}_{1}
have all desired properties.

Observe that Items (1) and (2) follow directly from the construction. For Item (4), we observe that every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}
is created in step (1) and hence, contains a node
\varphi\in\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}
and all of its neighbors (in
\mathcal{J}
) by the definition of
\mathcal{I}
. Thus,
|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\geq 2g/\epsilon
since
\varphi
has at least
2g/\epsilon
neighbors. For Item (3), we observe that each subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}
after step (3) has hop-diameter at least
2
and at most
6
. Thus,
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq 7g\epsilon L_{i}+6L_{i}\penalty\ \leq\penalty\ 13L_{i}
. Furthermore, since every edge
\mathbf{e}\in\mathcal{E}_{i}
has a weight of at least
L_{i}/(1+\psi)\geq L_{i}/2
and
\mathcal{X}
has at least two edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
,
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\geq 2(L_{i}/2)=L_{i}
. The construction time follows straightforwardly from the algorithm.

\Box

Given a tree
T
, we say that a node
x\in T
is
T
-branching
if it has degree at least 3 in
T
. For brevity, we shall omit the prefix
T
in “
T
-branching” whenever this does not lead to confusion. Given a forest
F
, we say that
x
is
F
-branching
if it is
T
-branching for some tree
T\subseteq F
. The construction of Step 2 is described in the following lemma.

Lemma 8.2

(Step 2)
.

Let
\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}
be the forest obtained from
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
by removing every node in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}+}_{i}
(defined in
Lemma

8.1
). We can construct in
O(|\mathcal{V}_{i}|)
time a collection
\mathbb{X}_{2}
of subtrees of
\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}
such that for every
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{2}
:

(1)

\mathcal{X}
is a tree and has an
\mathcal{X}
-branching node.

(2)

L_{i}\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq 2L_{i}
.

(3)

|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|=\Omega(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
when
\epsilon\leq 1/g
.

(4)

Let
\widetilde{F}^{(3)}_{i}
be obtained from
\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}
by removing every node contained in subgraphs of
\mathbb{X}_{2}
. Then, for every tree
\widetilde{T}\subseteq\widetilde{F}^{(3)}_{i}
, either (4a)
\mathsf{Adm}(\widetilde{T})\leq 6L_{i}
or (4b)
\widetilde{T}
is a path.

Proof:

We say that a tree
\widetilde{T}\in\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}
is
long
if
\mathsf{Adm}(\widetilde{T})\geq 6L_{i}
and
short
otherwise. We construct
\mathbb{X}_{2}
, initially empty, as follows:

•

Pick a long tree
\widetilde{T}
of
\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}
that has at least one
\widetilde{T}
-branching node, say
\varphi
. We traverse
\widetilde{T}
starting from
\varphi
and
truncate
the traversal at nodes whose augmented distance from
\varphi
is at least
L_{i}
, which will be the leaves of the subtree. (The exact implementation details are delayed until the end of this proof.)
As a result, the augmented radius (with respect to the center
\varphi
) of the subtree induced by the visited (non-truncated) nodes is at least
L_{i}
and at most
L_{i}+\bar{w}+g\epsilon L_{i}
. We then form a subgraph, say
\mathcal{X}
, from the subtree induced by the visited nodes, add
\mathcal{X}
to
\mathbb{X}_{2}
, remove every node of
\mathcal{X}
from
\widetilde{T}
, and repeat this step until it no longer applies.

We observe that Item (1) follows directly from the construction. Since the algorithm only stops when every long tree has no branching node, meaning that it is a path, Item (4) is satisfied. By construction,
\mathcal{X}
is a tree of augmented radius at least
L_{i}
and at most
L_{i}+g\epsilon L_{i}+\bar{w}
, hence
L_{i}\penalty\ \leq\penalty\ \mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\penalty\ \leq\penalty\ 2(L_{i}+g\epsilon L_{i}+\bar{w})\penalty\ \leq\penalty\ 6L_{i}
since
\bar{w}<L_{i}
and
\epsilon\leq 1/g
; this implies Item (2). Let
\mathcal{D}
be an augmented diameter path of
\mathcal{X}
;
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{D})\geq L_{i}
by construction. Note that every edge has a weight of at most
\bar{w}\leq L_{i-1}
and every node has a weight of in
[L_{i-1},gL_{i-1}]
by
property (P3’)
. Thus,
\mathcal{D}
has at least
\frac{\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{D})}{2gL_{i-1}}\penalty\ \geq\penalty\ \frac{L_{i}}{2g\epsilon L_{i}}=\Omega(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
nodes; this implies Item (3). The construction of
\mathbb{X}_{2}
can be implemented efficiently in
O(|\mathcal{V}_{i}|)
by simply maintaining a list
\mathcal{B}
of branching nodes of
\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}
.

\Box

The goal of constructing a subgraph from a branching node
\varphi
is to guarantee that there must be at least one neighbor, say
\varphi^{\prime}
, of
\varphi
that does not belong to the augmented diameter path of
\mathcal{X}
. Thus, we could show that the amount of corrected potential change
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})
is at least
\omega(\varphi^{\prime})\geq L_{i-1}=\epsilon L_{i}
. This will ultimately help us show that the corrected potential change
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})
is
\Omega(\epsilon^{2}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i})
.

Step 3: Augmenting
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}
.

Let
\widetilde{F}^{(3)}_{i}
be the forest obtained in Item (4b) in
Lemma

8.2
. Let
\mathcal{A}
be the set of all nodes
\varphi
in
\widetilde{F}^{(3)}_{i}
such that
\varphi
is in a tree
\widetilde{T}\in\widetilde{F}^{(3)}_{3}
of augmented diameter at least
6L_{i}
and
\varphi
is a branching node in
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
. For each node
\varphi\in\mathcal{A}
such that
\varphi
is connected to a node, say
\varphi^{\prime}
, in a subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}
via an
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edge
\mathbf{e}
, we add
\varphi
and
\mathbf{e}
to
\mathcal{X}
. We note that
\varphi^{\prime}
exists since
\varphi
has degree at least
3
in
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
. (If there are many such nodes
\varphi^{\prime}
, we choose an arbitrary one.)

The following lemma follows directly from the construction.

Lemma 8.3
.

The augmentation in Step 3 can be implemented in
O(|\mathcal{V}_{i}|)
time, and increases the augmented diameter of each subgraph in
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}
by at most
4L_{i}
when
\epsilon\leq 1/g
.

Furthermore, let
\widetilde{F}^{(4)}_{i}
be the forest obtained from
\widetilde{F}^{(3)}_{i}
by removing every node in
\mathcal{A}
. Then, for every tree
\widetilde{T}\subseteq\widetilde{F}^{(4)}_{i}
, either:

(1)

\mathsf{Adm}(\widetilde{T})\leq 6L_{i}
or

(2)

\widetilde{T}
is a path such that (2a) every node in
\widetilde{T}
has
degree at most
2
in
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
and (2b) at least one endpoint
\varphi
of
\widetilde{T}
is connected via an
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edge to a node
\varphi^{\prime}
in a subgraph of
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}
, unless
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}=\emptyset
.

The main intuition behind Step 3 is to guarantee properties (2a) and (2b) for every long path
\widetilde{T}\in\widetilde{F}^{(4)}_{i}
. Recall that in Item (3) of
Definition

6.15
, we guarantee that
\mathcal{G}_{i}
has no removable edge. Thus, any edge between two nodes in
\widetilde{T}
is not removable. Later, we use this property to argue that the corrected potential change
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})
is non-trivial for every subgraph
\mathcal{X}
formed in the construction of Step 4 below.

Required definitions/preparations for Step 4.

Let
\widetilde{F}^{(4)}_{i}
be the forest obtained from
\widetilde{F}^{(3)}_{i}
as described in
Lemma

8.3
. By Item (2b) in
Lemma

8.3
, every tree of augmented diameter at least
6L_{i}
of
\widetilde{F}^{(4)}_{i}
is a simple path, which we call a
long path
.

Red/Blue Coloring.
Given a path
\widetilde{P}\subseteq\widetilde{F}^{(4)}_{i}
, we color their nodes red or blue. If a node has augmented distance at most
L_{i}
from at least one of the path’s endpoints, we color it red; otherwise, we color it blue. Observe that each red node belongs to the suffix or prefix of
\mathcal{P}
; the other nodes are colored blue.

Lemma 8.4

(Step 4)
.

Let
\widetilde{F}^{(4)}_{i}
be the forest obtained from
\widetilde{F}^{(3)}_{i}
as described in
Lemma

8.3
. We can construct in
O((|\mathcal{V}_{i}|+|\mathcal{E}_{i}|)\epsilon^{-1})
time a collection
\mathbb{X}_{4}
of subgraphs of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
such that every
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{4}
:

(1)

\mathcal{X}
contains a single edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
.

(2)

L_{i}\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq 5L_{i}
.

(3)

|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|=\Theta(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
when
\epsilon\leq 1/(8(g+1))
.

(4)

\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(\epsilon^{2}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i})
.

(5)

Let
\widetilde{F}^{(5)}_{i}
be obtained from
\widetilde{F}^{(4)}_{i}
by removing every node contained in subgraphs of
\mathbb{X}_{4}
. If we apply
Red/Blue Coloring
to each path of augmented diameter at least
6L_{i}
in
\widetilde{F}^{(5)}_{i}
, then there is no edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
that connects two blue nodes in
\widetilde{F}^{(5)}_{i}
.

Proof:
We only apply the construction to paths of augmented diameter at least
6L_{i}
in
\widetilde{F}^{(4)}_{i}
, called
long paths
. Let
\widetilde{P}
be a long path. For each blue node
\varphi\in\widetilde{P}
, we assign a subpath
\mathcal{I}(\varphi)
of
\widetilde{P}
,
called the
interval of
\varphi
, which contains every node within an augmented distance (in
\widetilde{P}
) at most
L_{i}
from
\varphi
. By definition, we have:

Claim 8.5
.

For any blue node
\nu
, it holds that

(a)

(2-(3g+2)\epsilon)L_{i}\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{I}(\nu))\leq 2L_{i}
.

(b)

Denote by
\mathcal{I}_{1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{2}
the two subpaths obtained by removing
\nu
from the path
\mathcal{I}(\nu)
.
Each of these subpaths has
\Theta(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
nodes and augmented diameter at least
(1-2(g+1)\epsilon)L_{i}
.

We keep track of a list
\mathcal{B}
of edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
with both
blue endpoints
. We then construct
\mathbb{X}_{4}
, initially empty, as follows:

•

Pick an edge
(\nu,\mu)
with both blue endpoints, form a subgraph
\mathcal{X}=\{(\nu,\mu)\cup\mathcal{I}(\nu)\cup\mathcal{I}(\mu)\}
, and add
\mathcal{X}
to
\mathbb{X}_{4}
. We then remove all nodes in
\mathcal{I}_{\nu}\cup\mathcal{I}_{\mu}
from the path or two paths containing
\nu
and
\mu
, update the color of nodes in the new paths to satisfy
Red/Blue Coloring
and the edge set
\mathcal{B}
, and repeat this step until it no longer applies.

We observe that Items (1) and (5) follow directly from the construction. For Item (2), we observe by Claim
8.5
that
\mathcal{I}(v)
has augmented diameter at most
2L_{i}
and at least
L_{i}
when
\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{8(g+1)}
, and the weight of the edge
(\mu,\nu)
is at most
L_{i}
. Thus,
L_{i}\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq L_{i}+2\cdot 2L_{i}=5L_{i}
, as claimed. Item (3) follows directly from Claim
8.5
since
|\mathcal{I}(v)|=\Theta(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
and
|\mathcal{I}(\mu)|=\Theta(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
.

Next, we show that the construction of
\mathbb{X}_{4}
can be implemented efficiently. Since the interval
\mathcal{I}(\nu)
assigned to each blue node
\nu
consists of
O(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
nodes by Claim
8.5
(b), it takes
O(|\mathcal{E}_{i}|\epsilon^{-1})
time to construct
\mathcal{B}
. For each edge
(\nu,\mu)\in\mathcal{B}
picked in the construction of
\mathbb{X}_{4}
, forming
\mathcal{X}=\{(\nu,\mu)\cup\mathcal{I}(\nu)\cup\mathcal{I}(\mu)\}
takes
O(1)
time. When removing any such interval
\mathcal{I}(\nu)
from a path
\widetilde{P}
, we may create two new sub-paths
\widetilde{P}_{1},\widetilde{P}_{2}
, and then need to recolor the nodes following
Red/Blue Coloring
. Specifically, some blue nodes in the prefix and/or suffix of
\widetilde{P}_{1},\widetilde{P}_{2}
are colored red; importantly, a node’s color may only change from blue to red, but it may not change in the other direction. Since the total number of nodes to be recolored as a result of removing such an interval
\mathcal{I}(\nu)
is
O(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
, the total recoloring running time is
O(|\mathcal{V}(\widetilde{F}^{(4)}_{i})|\epsilon^{-1})=O(|\mathcal{V}_{i}|\epsilon^{-1})
.
To bound the time required for updating the edge set
\mathcal{B}
throughout this process, we
note that edges are never added to
\mathcal{B}
after its initiation. Specifically, when a blue node
\nu
is recolored as red, we remove all incident edges of
\nu
from
\mathcal{B}
, and none of these edges will be considered again; this can be done in
O(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
time per node
\nu
, since
\nu
is incident to at most
\frac{2g}{\epsilon}=O(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
due to the construction of Step 1 (
Lemma

8.1
). Once a node is added to
\mathcal{X}
, it will never be considered again. It follows that the total running time required for implementing Step 3 is
O(|\mathcal{V}_{i}|\epsilon^{-1})
, as claimed.

We now prove Item (4). We consider two cases.

Case 1:
\mathcal{I}(\nu)\cap\mathcal{I}(\mu)=\emptyset
.

Let
\mathcal{X}=(\nu,\mu)\cup\mathcal{I}(\nu)\cup\mathcal{I}(\mu)
where
\mathbf{e}=(\nu,\mu)
is the only edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
contained in
\mathcal{X}
. For any subgraph
\mathcal{Z}
of
\mathcal{X}
, we define:

\Phi^{+}(\mathcal{Z})=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{Z}}\omega(\alpha)+\sum_{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}\cap\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{Z})}\omega(\mathbf{e})

(39)

to be the total weight of nodes and
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edges in
\mathcal{Z}
. Let
\mathcal{D}
be an augmented diameter path of
\mathcal{X}
, and
\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{D})
be the subgraph obtained from
\mathcal{X}
by removing nodes on
\mathcal{D}
. Let
\mathcal{I}(\nu)
and
\mathcal{I}(\mu)
be two intervals in the construction on Step 4 that are connected by an edge
\mathbf{e}=(\nu,\mu)
.

Claim 8.6
.

\Phi^{+}(\mathcal{Y})=\frac{5L_{i}}{4}+\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{Y})|\epsilon L_{i})
.

Proof:

Let
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{Y}\setminus(\mathcal{I}(\nu)\cup\mathcal{I}(\mu))
be the subgraph of
\mathcal{Y}
obtained by removing every node in
\mathcal{I}(\nu)\cup\mathcal{I}(\mu)
from
\mathcal{Y}
, and
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{Y}\cap(\mathcal{I}(\nu)\cup\mathcal{I}(\mu))
be the subgraph of
\mathcal{Y}
induced by nodes of
\mathcal{Y}
in
(\mathcal{I}(\nu)\cup\mathcal{I}(\mu))
. Observe that
\Phi^{+}(\mathcal{A})\geq|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{A})|L_{i-1}=|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{A})|\epsilon L_{i}.
. If
\mathcal{D}
does not contain the edge
(\nu,\mu)
(see Figure
7
(a)), then
\mathcal{I}(\nu)\cap\mathcal{D}=\emptyset
, say, which implies
\Phi^{+}(\mathcal{B})\geq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{I}(\nu))\geq(2-(3g+2)\epsilon)L_{i}
by Claim
8.5
. If
D
contains the edge
(\nu,\mu)
(see Figure
7
(b)), then at least two sub-intervals, say
\mathcal{I}_{1},\mathcal{I}_{2}
, are disjoint from
\mathcal{D}
.
By Claim
8.5
,
\Phi^{+}(\mathcal{B})\geq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{I}_{1})+\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{I}_{2})\geq(2-4(g+1)\epsilon)L_{i}
. In both cases,
\Phi^{+}(\mathcal{B})\geq(2-4(g+1)\epsilon)L_{i}\geq\frac{3L_{i}}{2}
when
\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{8(g+1)}
. Thus:

\begin{split}\Phi^{+}(\mathcal{Y})&\geq\Phi^{+}(\mathcal{A})+\frac{3L_{i}}{2}\penalty\ =\penalty\ \frac{5L_{i}}{4}+\Omega((|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{A})|+|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{B})|)\epsilon L_{i})=\frac{5L_{i}}{4}+\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{Y})|\epsilon L_{i}),\end{split}

which concludes the proof of Claim
8.6
.

\Box

Figure 7:
\mathcal{D}
is the diameter path and enclosed trees are augmented to a Step-4 subgraph in Step 5A. The green shaded regions contain nodes in
\mathcal{D}
. (a)
\mathcal{D}
does not contain
\mathbf{e}
. (b)
\mathcal{D}
contains
\mathbf{e}
.

Note that
\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{D})\leq\frac{gL_{i}}{L_{i-1}}=O(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
since every node has weight at least
L_{i-1}
by
property (P3’)
. Thus, we have:

\begin{split}\Delta_{i}^{+}(\mathcal{X})&=\Phi^{+}(\mathcal{D})+\Phi^{+}(\mathcal{Y})-\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})=\Phi(\mathcal{Y})-\omega(\mathbf{e})\\
&\geq L_{i}/4+\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{Y})|\epsilon L_{i})\qquad\mbox{(by Claim\penalty\ \ref{clm:PotentialY-bound})}\\
&=\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{D})|\epsilon L_{i})+\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{Y})|\epsilon L_{i})=\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon L_{i})\penalty\ .\end{split}

Thus, Item (4) of
Lemma

8.4
follows.

Case 2:
\mathcal{I}(\nu)\cap\mathcal{I}(\mu)\not=\emptyset
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a diameter path of
\mathcal{X}
, and
\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{D})
. Recall that
\mathcal{X}
contains only one edge
\mathbf{e}=(\nu,\mu)\in\mathcal{E}_{i}
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{e}}=(\nu,\mathbf{e},\mu)
be the path that consists of only edge
\mathbf{e}
and its endpoints. Let
\mathcal{P}[\nu,\mu]
be the subpath of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
between
\nu
and
\mu
.

Figure 8:
Nodes enclosed in dashed red curves are augmented to
\mathcal{X}
in Step 4.

We observe that
\mathbf{e}
is not removable by Item (3) of
Definition

6.15
. Then it follows that:

\begin{split}\omega(\mathcal{P}[\nu,\mu])-\omega(\mathcal{P}_{e}))&>6g\epsilon\cdot\omega(\mathbf{e})-w(\nu)-w(\mu)\\
&>6g\epsilon L_{i}/2-2g\epsilon L_{i}=g\epsilon L_{i}\end{split}

(40)

In particular, this means that
\omega(\mathcal{P}(\nu,\mu))\geq\omega(\mathbf{e})
.

Thus, if
\mathcal{D}
contains both
\nu
and
\mu
, then it must contain
\mathbf{e}
, since otherwise,
\mathcal{D}
must contain
\mathcal{P}[\nu,\mu]
and by replacing
\mathcal{P}[\nu,\mu]
by
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{e}}
we obtain a shorter path by Equation (
40
) (see Figure
8
). Observe that

Observation 8.7
.

|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{P}[\nu,\mu])|\leq\frac{4}{\epsilon}
and
|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{D})|\leq\frac{g}{\epsilon}
.

We consider two cases:

•

Case 1
If
\mathcal{D}
does not contain edge
\mathbf{e}
, then (a)
\mathcal{D}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
and (b)
|\{\nu,\mu\}\cap D|\leq 1
. From (a) and
8.7
, we have:

\begin{split}\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})&\geq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{D})+\Phi^{+}(\mathcal{Y})-\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})=\Phi^{+}(\mathcal{Y})\\
&\geq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{P}[\mu,\nu])+\Phi^{+}(\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{P}[\mu,\nu])\\
&\geq w(\mathbf{e})+|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{P}[\mu,\nu])|L_{i-1}\geq L_{i}/2+|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{P}[\mu,\nu])|\epsilon L_{i}\\
&=\Omega(\epsilon(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{P}[\mu,\nu])|+|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{D})|)L_{i})+|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{P}[\mu,\nu])|\epsilon L_{i}=\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon L_{i})\end{split}

(41)

•

Case 2
If
\mathcal{D}
contains
\mathbf{e}
, then
\mathcal{D}\cap\mathcal{P}(\nu,\mu)=\emptyset
; here
\mathcal{P}(\nu,\mu)
is the path obtained from
\mathcal{P}[\nu,\mu]
by removing its endpoints. It follows that

\begin{split}\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})&\geq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{D})+\Phi^{+}(\mathcal{Y})-\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})=\Phi(\mathcal{Y})-w(\mathbf{e})\\
&\geq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{P}[\mu,\nu])+\Phi^{+}(\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{P}[\mu,\nu])-w(\mathbf{e})\\
&\geq g\epsilon L_{i}+|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{P}[\mu,\nu])|L_{i-1}\qquad\mbox{(by Equation\penalty\ \eqref{eq:p-vs-pe})}\\
&=\Omega((|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{P}[\mu,\nu])|+|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{D})|)\epsilon^{2}L_{i})+|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{P}[\mu,\nu])|\epsilon L_{i}=\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon^{2}L_{i})\end{split}

(42)

In both cases, we have
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon^{2}L_{i})
as claimed in Item (4) of
Lemma

8.4
.

\Box

Observation 8.8
.

For every tree
\widetilde{T}\subseteq\widetilde{F}^{(5)}_{i}
such that
\mathsf{Adm}(\widetilde{T})\leq 6L_{i}
, then
\widetilde{T}
is connected via
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edge to a node in some subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}
, unless there is no subgraph formed in Steps 1-4, i.e,
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}=\emptyset
.

We call the case where
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}=\emptyset
the
degenerate case
. When the degenerate case happens,
\mathcal{G}_{i}
has a very special structure, which will be described later (in
Lemma

8.11
); for now, we focus on the construction of the last step.

Step 5.

Let
\widetilde{T}
be a path in
\widetilde{F}^{(5)}_{i}
obtained by Item (5) of
Lemma

8.4
. We construct two sets of subgraphs, denoted by
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}_{5}
and
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{pref}}_{5}
, of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
. The construction is broken into two steps. Step 5A is only applicable when the degenerate case does not happen; Step 5B is applicable regardless of the degenerate case.

•

(Step 5A)
If
\widetilde{T}
has augmented diameter at most
6L_{i}
, let
\mathbf{e}
be an
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edge connecting
\widetilde{T}
and a node in some subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}
;
\mathbf{e}
exists by
8.8
. We add both
\mathbf{e}
and
\widetilde{T}
to
\mathcal{X}
.

•

(Step 5B)
Otherwise, the augmented diameter of
\widetilde{T}
is at least
6L_{i}
and hence, it must be a path by Item (4) in
Lemma

8.2
. In this case, we greedily break
\widetilde{T}
into subpaths of augmented diameter at least
L_{i}
and at most
2L_{i}
. Let
\widetilde{P}
be a subpath broken from
\widetilde{T}
. If
\widetilde{P}
is connected to a node in a subgraph
\mathcal{X}
via an edge
\mathbf{e}\in\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
, we add
\widetilde{P}
and
\mathbf{e}
to
\mathcal{X}
. If
\widetilde{P}
contains an endpoint of
\widetilde{T}
, we add
\widetilde{P}
to
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{pref}}_{5}
; otherwise, we add
\widetilde{P}
to
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}_{5}
.

Lemma 8.9
.

We can implement the construction of
\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}
and
\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
in
O(|\mathcal{V}_{i}|)
time. Furthermore, every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}\cup\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
satisfies:

(1)

\mathcal{X}
is a subpath of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
.

(2)

L_{i}\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq 2L_{i}
when
\epsilon\leq 1/g
.

(3)

|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|=\Theta(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
.

Proof:

Items (1) and (2) follow directly from the construction. For Item (3), we observe the following facts:
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\geq L_{i}
, each edge has a weight of at most
L_{i-1}
, and each node has a weight of at most
gL_{i-1}
. Thus,
|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\geq\frac{L_{i}}{(1+g)L_{i-1}}=\Omega(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
. By the same argument, since each node has a weight at least
L_{i-1}
by
property (P3’)
,
|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\leq\frac{2L_{i}}{L_{i-1}}=O(1/\epsilon)
. The construction time follows by implementing the algorithm greedily.

Finally, we construct the collection
\mathbb{X}
of subgraphs of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
as follows:

\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}\cup\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}\cup\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}.

(43)

To complete the proof of
Lemma

7.3
, we need to:

1.

show that subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}
satisfies three properties:
(P1’)
,
(P2’)
, and
(P3’)
, and that
|\mathcal{E}_{i}\cap\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})|=O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
. This implies Item (5) of
Lemma

7.3
. We present the proof in
Section

8.1
.

2.

construct a partition
\{\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathsf{high}},\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathsf{low}^{+}},\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}\}
of
\mathcal{V}_{i}
, show Items (1)-(4) and the running time bound as claimed by
Lemma

7.3
. We present the proof in
Section

8.2

8.1
Properties of
\mathbb{X}

In this section, we prove the following lemma.

Lemma 8.10
.

Let
\mathbb{X}
be the set of subgraph as defined in
Equation

43
. For every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
,
\mathcal{X}
satisfies the three properties (
P1’
)-(
P3’
) with
g=31
and
\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{8(g+1)}
, and
|\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}|=O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
. Furthermore,
\mathbb{X}
can be constructed in
O((|\mathcal{V}_{i}|+|\mathcal{E}_{i}|)\epsilon^{-1})
time.

Proof:
We observe that
property (P1’)
follows directly from the construction. Additionally,
property (P2’)
follows from Item (4) of
Lemma

8.1
, Items (3) of
Lemma

8.2
,
Lemma

8.4
, and
Lemma

8.9
. The lower bound
L_{i}
on the augmented diameter of a subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
follows from Item (3) of
Lemma

8.1
, Items (2) of
Lemma

8.2
,
Lemma

8.4
, and
Lemma

8.9
. Thus, to complete the proof of
property (P3’)
, it remains to show that
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq gL_{i}
with
g=31
and
\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{8(g+1)}
. Observe that the condition that
\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{8(g+1)}
follows by considering all constraints on
\epsilon
in
Lemmas

8.1
,
8.2
,
8.3
,
8.4
and
8.9
.

If
\mathcal{X}
is formed in Step 5B, that is
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}_{5}\cup\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{pref}}_{5}
, then
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\penalty\ \leq\penalty\ 2L_{i}
by
Lemma

8.9
. Otherwise, excluding any augmentation to
\mathcal{X}
due to Step 5,
Lemma

8.1
,
Lemma

8.2
and
Lemma

8.3
yield
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq 13L_{i}+4L_{i}\leq 17L_{i}
where
+4L_{i}
is due to the augmentation in Step 3 (see
Lemma

8.3
). By
Lemma

8.4
,
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq\max(17L_{i},5L_{i})=17L_{i}
.

We then may augment
\mathcal{X}
with trees of diameter at most
6L_{i}
(Step 5A) and/or with subpaths of diameter at most
2L_{i}
(Step 5B). As the augmentation is star-like and via
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edges, if we denote the resulting subgraph by
\mathcal{X}^{+}
, then

\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X}^{+})\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})+2\bar{w}+12L_{i}\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})+14L_{i}\leq 31L_{i}.

Property (P3’)
now follows.

The fact that
|\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}|=O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
and the running time bound follow directly from
Lemma

8.1
,
Lemma

8.2
,
Lemma

8.3
,
Lemma

8.4
and
Lemma

8.9
. Recall that the augmentation in Step 3 is in a star-like way and hence, no cycle is formed in subgraphs of
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}
after the augmentation.

\Box

8.2
Constructing a Partition of
\mathcal{V}_{i}

We first consider the degenerate case where
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}=\emptyset
.

Lemma 8.11

(Structure of Degenerate Case)
.

If
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}=\emptyset
, then

\widetilde{F}^{(5)}_{i}=\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
, and
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
is a single (long) path. Moreover, every edge
\mathbf{e}\in\mathcal{E}_{i}
must be incident to a node in
\widetilde{P}_{1}\cup\widetilde{P}_{2}
,
where
\widetilde{P}_{1}
and
\widetilde{P}_{2}
are the prefix and suffix subpaths of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
of augmented diameter at most
L_{i}
. Consequently, we have that
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|=O(1/\epsilon^{2})
.

Proof:
By the assumption of the lemma, no subgraph is formed in Steps 1-4.

Figure 9:
Red edges are edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
; every edge is incident to at least one red node.

Since no subgraph is formed in Step 1,
\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}=\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
. Since no subgraph is formed in Step 2, there is no branching node in
\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}
; thus
\widetilde{F}^{(3)}_{i}=\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}
and it is a single (long) path. Since
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}=\emptyset
, there is no augmentation in Step 3. Since no subgraph is formed in Step 4,
\widetilde{F}^{(5)}_{i}=\widetilde{F}^{(4)}_{i}
and both are equal to
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
, which is a long path (see Figure
9
).

By Item (5) in
Lemma

8.4
, any edge
\mathbf{e}\in\mathcal{E}_{i}
must be incident to a red node. The augmented distance from any red node to at least one endpoint of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
is at most
L_{i}
by the definition of
Red/Blue Coloring
, and hence every red node belongs to
\widetilde{P}_{1}\cup\widetilde{P}_{2}
. Since each node has a weight of at least
L_{i-1}
by
property (P3’)
, we have:

|\mathcal{V}(\widetilde{P}_{1}\cup\widetilde{P}_{2})|\leq\frac{2L_{i}}{L_{i-1}}=\frac{2}{\epsilon}

Since each node of
\widetilde{P}_{1}\cup\widetilde{P}_{2}
is incident to at most
\frac{2g}{\epsilon}
edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
(as there is no subgraph formed in Step 1;
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}=\emptyset
), it holds that
|\mathcal{E}_{i}|=O(1/\epsilon^{2})
, as desired.

\Box

We are now ready to describe the construction of the partition
\{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i},\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{+}}_{i},\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}_{i}\}
of
\mathcal{V}_{i}

Construct Partition
\{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i},\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{+}}_{i},\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}_{i}\}
:
If the degenerate case happens, we define
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}_{i}=\mathcal{V}_{i}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{+}}_{i}=\emptyset
. Otherwise, we define
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}
to be the set of all nodes that are incident to at least
2g/\epsilon
edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
,
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}_{i}=\cup_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}_{5}}\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})
and
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{+}}_{i}=\mathcal{V}_{i}\setminus(\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}\cup\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}_{i})
.

We show the following property of
\{\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i},\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{+}}_{i},\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}_{i}\}
, which is equivalent to Item (4) in
Lemma

7.3
.

Lemma 8.12
.

(1)

If
\mathcal{X}
contains a node in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}
, then
\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})\subseteq\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}
.

(2)

There is no edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
between a node in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}
and a node in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}_{i}
.

(3)

If there exists an edge
(\varphi_{C_{u}},\varphi_{C_{v}})\in\mathcal{E}_{i}
such that both
\varphi_{C_{u}}
and
\varphi_{C_{v}}
are in

\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}
, then the degenerate case happens.

Proof:

Item (1) follows directly from the construction. By the construction of Step 1 (
Lemma

8.1
), any neighbor, say
\varphi
, of a node in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}
is in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}+}_{i}
. Thus,
\varphi
will not be considered after Step 1. It follows that there is no edge between a node in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}
and a node in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}_{i}
since nodes in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}_{i}
are in Step 5; Item (2) follows. To show Item (3), we observe that every node, say
\varphi_{C_{u}}
, in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}
is a blue node of some long path
\widetilde{P}
in
\widetilde{F}^{(5)}_{i}
. If the degenerate case does not happen, then by Item (5) of
Lemma

8.4
, every edge
(\varphi_{C_{u}},\varphi_{C_{v}})
must have the node
\varphi_{C_{v}}
being a red node of
\widetilde{P}
. But then by the construction of Step
5B
,
\varphi_{C_{v}}
belongs to some subgraph of
\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
and hence is not in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}
.

\Box

Next, we focus on bounding the corrected potential change
\Delta^{+}_{i}(\mathcal{X})
of every cluster
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
. Specifically, we show that:

•

if
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}
, then
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i}\epsilon)
; the proof is in
Lemma

8.13
.

•

if
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{2}
, then
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i}\epsilon^{2})
; the proof is in
Lemma

8.14
.

•

if
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{4}
, then
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i}\epsilon^{2})
; the proof is in
Lemma

8.15
.

•

the corrected potential change is non-negative and we provide a lower bound of the average corrected potential change for subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}\setminus\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}
in
Lemma

8.16
.

Lemma 8.13
.

For every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}
, it holds that
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})\geq\frac{|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i}\epsilon}{2}
.

Proof:

Let
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}
be a subgraph formed in Step 1. By Item (4) of
Lemma

8.1
,
|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\geq\frac{2g}{\epsilon}
. By definition of
\Delta^{i}_{L}(\mathcal{X})
(
Lemma

7.3
), we have:

\begin{split}\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})&\geq\sum_{\varphi\in\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})}\omega(\varphi)-\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\hyperlink{P3'}{\tiny{(P3')}}}}}{{\geq}}\sum_{\varphi\in\mathcal{X}}L_{i-1}-gL_{i}=\frac{|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i-1}}{2}+\underbrace{(\frac{|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i-1}}{2}-gL_{i})}_{\geq 0\mbox{ since }|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\geq(2g)/\epsilon}\\
&\geq\frac{|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i-1}}{2}=\frac{|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon L_{i}}{2},\end{split}

(44)

as claimed.

\Box

Lemma 8.14
.

For every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{2}
, it holds that

\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})\ =\Omega\left(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i}\epsilon^{2}\right)
.

Proof:

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a subgraph that is initially formed in Step 2 and could possibly be augmented in Steps 3 and 5. Recall that in the augmentation done in Step 3, we add to
\mathcal{X}
nodes of
\mathcal{V}_{i}
via
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edges, and in the augmentation done in Step 5, we add to
\mathcal{X}
subtrees of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
via
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edges. Thus, the resulting subgraph after the augmentation remains, as prior to the augmentation, a subtree of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
. That is,
\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\subseteq\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
. Letting
\mathcal{D}
be an augmented diameter path of
\mathcal{X}
, we have by definition of augmented diameter that

\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})=\sum_{\varphi\in\mathcal{D}}\omega(\varphi)+\sum_{\mathbf{e}\in\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{D})}\omega(e)

Let
\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})\setminus\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{D})
. Then
|\mathcal{Y}|>0
since
\mathcal{X}
has a
\mathcal{X}
-branching node by Item (1) of
Lemma

8.2
and that

\begin{split}\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\left(\sum_{\varphi\in\mathcal{X}}\omega(\varphi)+\sum_{e\in\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})}\omega(e)\right)-\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\geq\sum_{\varphi\in\mathcal{Y}}\omega(\varphi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\hyperlink{P3'}{\tiny{(P3')}}}}}{{\geq}}|\mathcal{Y}|L_{i-1}\end{split}

(45)

As
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{D})\leq gL_{i}
, it holds that
|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{D})|=O(1/\epsilon)=O(\frac{|\mathcal{Y}|}{\epsilon})
. Thus,

\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})\geq\frac{|\mathcal{Y}|L_{i-1}}{2}+\Omega(\epsilon|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{D})|L_{i-1})=\Omega((|\mathcal{Y}|+\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{D}))\epsilon L_{i-1})=\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon^{2}L_{i}),

as claimed.

\Box

Lemma 8.15
.

For every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{4}
, it holds that

\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega\left(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i}\epsilon^{2}\right)
.

Proof:
Let
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{4}
be a subgraph initially formed in Step 4;
\mathcal{X}
is possibly augmented in Step 5. Let
\mathcal{X}^{+}
be
\mathcal{X}
after the augmentation (if any). Let
\mathcal{D}^{+}
be the augmented diameter path of
\mathcal{X}^{+}
and
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}^{+}\cap\mathcal{X}
. By the same argument in
Lemma

8.14
,

\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon^{2}L_{i})=\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})\cup\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{D}^{+})|\epsilon^{2}L_{i}).

(46)

Furthermore,

\begin{split}\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X}^{+})&=\sum_{\varphi\in\mathcal{X}^{+}}\omega(\varphi)+\sum_{\mathbf{e}\in\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X}^{+})\cap\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}}\omega(\mathbf{e})-\omega(\mathcal{D}^{+})\\
&\geq\sum_{\varphi\in\mathcal{Y}}\omega(\varphi)+\sum_{\varphi\in\mathcal{X}}\omega(\varphi)+\sum_{\mathbf{e}\in\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}}\omega(\mathbf{e})-\omega(\mathcal{D})\\
&\geq\Omega(L_{i}\epsilon|\mathcal{Y}|)+\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny{\penalty\ \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:Step4PotentialXplus}}}}}{{=}}\Omega(|\mathcal{Y}|\epsilon L_{i})+\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})\cup\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{D}^{+})|\epsilon^{2}L_{i})\\
&=\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})\cup\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{D}^{+})\cup\mathcal{Y}|\epsilon^{2}L_{i})=\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X}^{+})|\epsilon^{2}L_{i}),\\
\end{split}

as claimed.

\Box

Next, we show Item (3) of
Lemma

7.3
regarding the corrected potential changes of subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}
.

Lemma 8.16
.

\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})\geq 0
for every
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
, and

\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}\setminus\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}}\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})=\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}\setminus\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}}\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon^{2}L_{i}).

Proof:

If
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}
, then
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})\geq 0
by
Lemmas

8.13
,
8.14
and
8.15
. Otherwise,
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{pref}}_{5}\cup\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}_{5}
, and hence is a subpath of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
. Thus, by definition,
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\sum_{\varphi\in\mathcal{X}}\omega(\varphi)+\sum_{\mathbf{e}\in\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}}\omega(\mathbf{e})-\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})=0
. That is,
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})\geq 0
in every case.

We now show a lower bound on the average potential change of subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}\setminus\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}
. We assume that the degenerate case does not happen; otherwise,
\mathbb{X}\setminus\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}=\emptyset
and there is nothing to prove. By Item (1) of
Lemma

8.12
,
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}=\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}_{5}
and only subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{pref}}_{5}
may not have positive potential change. By
Lemmas

8.13
,
8.14
and
8.15
, on average, each node
\varphi
in any subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}
has
\Omega(\epsilon^{2}L_{i})
corrected potential change, denoted by
\overline{\Delta}(\varphi)
.

By construction, a subgraph in
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{pref}}_{5}
is a prefix (or suffix), say
\widetilde{P}_{1}
, of a long path
\widetilde{P}
. The other suffix, say
\widetilde{P}_{2}
, of
\widetilde{P}
is augmented to a subgraph, say
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}
by the construction of Step
5BFast
and Item (2)
Lemma

8.3
. Since
|\mathcal{V}(\widetilde{P}_{2})|=\Omega(1/\epsilon)
by Item (3) of
Lemma

8.9
,
\sum_{\varphi\in\widetilde{P}_{2}}\overline{\Delta}(\varphi)=\Omega(1/\epsilon)(\epsilon^{2}L_{i})=\Omega(\epsilon L_{i})
. We distribute half this corrected potential change to all the nodes in
\widetilde{P}_{1}
, by Item (3) of
Lemma

8.9
, each gets
\Omega(\frac{\epsilon L_{i}}{1/\epsilon})=\Omega(\epsilon^{2}L_{i})
. This implies:

\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}\setminus\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}}\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})=\sum_{\varphi\in\mathcal{V}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}_{i}}\Omega(\epsilon^{2}L_{i})=\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}\setminus\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}}\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon^{2}L_{i}),

as desired.

\Box

We are now ready to prove
Lemma

7.3
.

Proof:
[Proof of
Lemma

7.3
]
We observe that Items (1), (2) and (4) follow directly
Lemma

8.11
and
Lemma

8.12
. Item (5) follows from
Lemma

8.10
. Item (3) follows from
Lemma

8.16
. The construction time is asymptotically the same as the construction time of
\mathbb{X}
, which is
O((|\mathcal{V}_{i}|+|\mathcal{E}_{i}|)\epsilon^{-1})
by
Lemma

8.10
.

Finally, we compute the augmented diameter of each subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
. We observe that the augmentations in Step 3 and Step 5 do not create any cycle. Thus, if
\mathcal{X}
is initially formed in Steps 1, 2 or 5B, then finally
\mathcal{X}
is a tree. It follows that the augmented diameter of
\mathcal{X}
can be computed in
O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
time by a simple tree traversal. If
\mathcal{X}
is formed in Step 4, then it has exactly one edge
\mathbf{e}
not in
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
by Item (1) in
Lemma

8.4
and that
\mathcal{X}
contains at most one cycle. Let
\mathcal{Z}
be such a cycle (if any);
\mathcal{Z}
has
O(1/\epsilon)
edges by Item (3) in
Lemma

8.4
. Thus, we can reduce computing the diameter of
\mathcal{X}
to computing the diameter of trees by guessing an edge of
\mathcal{Z}
that does not belong to the diameter path of
\mathcal{X}
and remove this edge from
\mathcal{X}
; the resulting graph is a tree. There are
O(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
guesses and each for each guess, computing the diameter takes
O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
time, which implies
O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon^{-1})
time to compute
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})
4
4

4

It is possible to compute the augmented diameter of
\mathcal{X}
in
O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
time using a more involved approach.
. Thus, the total running time is
\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}}O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon^{-1})=O(|\mathcal{V}_{i}|\epsilon^{-1})
.

\Box

9
Light Spanners for Minor-free Graphs in Linear Time

In
Section

4
, we show a construction of a light spanner for
K_{r}
-minor-free graphs with running time
O(nr\sqrt{r}\alpha(nr\sqrt{r},n))
. The extra factor
\alpha(nr\sqrt{r},n)
is due to
Union-Find
data structure in the proof of
Theorem

1.10
. To remove this factor, we do not use
Union-Find
. Instead, we follow the idea of Mareš
[
52
]
that was applied to construct a minimum spanning tree for
K_{r}
-minor-free graphs. Specifically, after the construction of level-
(i+1)
clusters, we prune the set of edges that are involved in the construction of levels at least
i+1
, which is
\cup_{j\geq i+1}E^{\sigma}_{j}
, as follows.

Let
E^{\sigma}_{\geq i}=\cup_{j\geq i}E^{\sigma}_{j}
. We inductively maintain a set of edges
\mathcal{E}_{\geq i}
, where each edge in
\mathcal{E}_{\geq i}
corresponds to an edge in
E^{\sigma}_{\geq i}
. (Note that only those in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
are involved in the construction of spanner at level
i
.) Furthermore, we inductively guarantee that
|\mathcal{E}_{\geq i}|=O(r\sqrt{\log r})|\mathcal{V}_{i}|
; we call this the
size invariant
. Upon completing the construction of level-
(i+1)
clusters, we construct the set of nodes
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}
. We now consider the set of edges
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\geq i+1}=\mathcal{E}_{\geq i}\setminus\mathcal{E}
. Let
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\geq i+1}
be obtained from
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\geq i+1}
by removing parallel edges: two edges
(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})
and
(\varphi^{\prime}_{1},\varphi^{\prime}_{2})
are
parallel
if there exist two subgraphs
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}\in\mathbb{X}
such that, w.l.o.g,
\varphi_{1},\varphi_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})
and
\varphi_{2},\varphi_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{Y})
. (Among all parallel edges, we keep the edge with minimum weight in
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i+1}
.) We construct the edge set
\mathcal{E}_{\geq i+1}
(between vertices in
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}
) at level
(i+1)
from
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\geq i+1}
by creating one edge
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})\in\mathcal{E}_{\geq i+1}
for each corresponding edge
(\varphi_{x},\varphi_{y})\in\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\geq i+1}
where
\varphi_{x}\in\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})
and
\varphi_{y}\in\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{Y})
;
\omega(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})=\omega(\varphi_{x},\varphi_{y})
.

Observe that
\mathcal{E}_{i+1}
corresponds to a subset of edges of
E^{\sigma}_{\geq i+1}
since
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\geq i+1}
, by definition, corresponds to a subset of edges of
E^{\sigma}_{\geq i+1}
. The stretch is in check (at most
(1+O(\epsilon))
), since we only remove parallel edges and that level-
(i+1)
clusters have diameter
O(\epsilon)
times the weight of level-
(i+1)
edges by
property (P3)
. Furthermore, since
\mathcal{E}_{\geq i}=O(r\sqrt{\log r}|\mathcal{V}_{i}|)
by the size invariant, the construction of
\mathcal{E}_{i+1}
can be done in
O(|\mathcal{V}_{i}|)
time. Since the graph
(\mathcal{V}_{i+1},\mathcal{E}_{\geq i+1})
is a minor of
G
and hence, is
K_{r}
-minor-free, we conclude that
|\mathcal{E}_{\geq i+1}|=O(r\sqrt{\log r})|\mathcal{V}_{i+1}|
by the sparsity of minor-free graphs, which implies the size invariant for level
i+1
.

By the size invariant, we do not need
Union-Find
data structure, as
\mathcal{E}_{\geq i}
now has
O(r\sqrt{\log r}|\mathcal{V}_{i}|)=O(r\sqrt{\log r}|\mathcal{C}_{i}|)
edges. Thus, the running time to construct
\mathcal{G}_{i}
in
Lemma

7.2
becomes
O_{\epsilon}(|\mathcal{C}_{i}|+|\mathcal{E}_{i}|)=O_{\epsilon}(r\sqrt{\log r}|\mathcal{C}_{i}|)
, and the running time to construct
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i+1}
in
Lemma

7.6
also becomes
O((r\sqrt{\log r}|\mathcal{C}_{i}|)
. The rest of the proof is the same as the proof in
Section

4.3
.

10
Fine-Grained Optimal Lightness: Proof of
Theorem

1.10
(2)

Our goal is to construct a cluster graph
\mathcal{G}_{i}
and a collection
\mathbb{X}
of subgraphs of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
satisfying properties
(P1’)
-
(P3’)
. We set
\psi=1/250
where
\psi
is the parameter in
Equation

22
.

By
Lemma

6.17
, the set of level-
(i+1)
obtained from subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}
obtained by applying the transformation in
Equation

26
will satisfy properties
(P1)
-
(P3)
. To be able to bound the set of edges in
H_{i}
(constructed in
Sections

11
and
12
), we need to guarantee that subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}
have sufficiently large potential changes. This indeed is the crux of our construction. We assume that
\epsilon>0
is a sufficiently small constant, i.e.,
\epsilon\ll 1,\epsilon=\Omega(1)
.

Constructing
\mathcal{G}_{i}
.

We shall assume inductively on
i,i\geq 1
that:

•

The set of edges
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
is given by the construction of the previous level
i
in the hierarchy; for the base case
i=1
(see
Section

6.2
),
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{1}
is simply a set of edges of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}
that are not in any level-
1
cluster.

•

The weight
\omega(\varphi_{C})
on each node
\varphi_{C}\in\mathcal{V}_{i}
is the potential value of cluster
C\in\mathcal{C}_{i}
; for the base case
i=1
, the potential values of level-
1
clusters were set in
Equation

25
.

After completing the construction of
\mathbb{X}
, we can compute the weight of each node of
\mathcal{G}_{i+1}
by computing the augmented diameter of each subgraph in
\mathcal{X}
; the running time is clearly polynomial. By the
end of this section
, we show to compute the spanning tree
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i+1}
for
\mathcal{G}_{i+1}
for the construction of the next level.

Realization of a path.

Let
\mathcal{P}=(\varphi_{0},(\varphi_{0},\varphi_{1}),\varphi_{1},(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}),\ldots,\varphi_{p})
be a path of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
, written as an alternating sequence of vertices and edges. Let
C_{i}
be the cluster corresponding to
\varphi_{i}
,
0\leq i\leq p
. Let
u
and
v
be two vertices such that
u
is in the cluster corresponding to
\varphi_{0}
and
v
is in the cluster corresponding to
\varphi_{p}
. See
Figure

6
for an illustration.

Let
\{y_{i}\}_{i=0}^{p}
and
\{z_{i}\}_{i=0}^{p}
be sequences of vertices of
G
such that (a)
z_{0}=u
and
y_{p}=v
and (b)
(y_{i-1},z_{i})
is the edge on
G
corresponds to edge
(\varphi_{i-1},\varphi_{i})
in
\mathcal{P}
for
1\leq i\leq p
. Let
Q_{i}
,
0\leq i\leq p
, be a shortest path in
H_{<L_{i-1}}[C_{i}]
between
z_{i}
and
y_{i}
where
C_{i}
is the cluster corresponding to
\varphi_{i}
. Let
P=Q_{0}\circ(y_{0},z_{1})\circ\ldots\circ Q_{p}
be a (possibly non-simple) path from
u
to
v
. We call
P
a
realization of
\mathcal{P}
with respect to
u
and
v
. The following observation follows directly from the definition of the weight function of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
.

Observation 10.1
.

Let
P
be a realization of
\mathcal{P}
w.r.t two vertices
u
and
v
. Then
w(P)\leq\omega(\mathcal{P})
.

Next, we show that to construct
H_{i}
, it suffices to focus on the edges of
E^{\sigma}_{i}
that correspond to edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
.

Lemma 10.2
.

Let
\psi=1/250
. We can construct a cluster graph

\mathcal{G}_{i}=(\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathcal{E}_{i}\cup\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i},\omega)
in polynomial time
such that
\mathcal{G}_{i}
satisfies all properties in
Definition

6.15
. Furthermore, let
F^{\sigma}_{i}
be the set of edges in
E^{\sigma}_{i}
that correspond to
\mathcal{E}_{i}
. If every edge in
F^{\sigma}_{i}
has a stretch
t(1+s\cdot\epsilon)
in
H_{<L_{i}}
for some constant
s\geq 1
, then every edge in
E^{\sigma}_{i}
has stretch
t(1+(2s+16g+1)\epsilon)
when
\epsilon<\frac{1}{2(12g+1)}
.

Proof:
Since
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
is given at the outset of the construction of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
, we only focus on constructing
\mathcal{E}_{i}
. For each edge
e=(u,v)\in E^{\sigma}_{i}
, we add an edge
(\varphi_{C_{u}},\varphi_{C_{v}})
to
\mathcal{G}_{i}
. Next, we remove edges from
\mathcal{G}_{i}
. (Step 1) we remove self-loops and parallel edges from
\mathcal{G}_{i}
; we only keep the edge of minimum weight in
\mathcal{G}_{i}
among parallel edges. (Step 2) If
t\geq 2
, we remove every edge
(\varphi_{C_{u}},\varphi_{C_{v}})
from
\mathcal{G}_{i}
such that
\omega(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}[\varphi_{C_{u}},\varphi_{C_{v}}])\leq t(1+6g\epsilon)\omega(\varphi_{C_{u}},\varphi_{C_{v}})
; the remaining edges of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
not in
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i+1}
are
\mathcal{E}_{i}
. If
t=1+\epsilon
, we apply the
\mathsf{path\penalty\ greedy}
algorithm to
\mathcal{G}_{i}
with stretch
t(1+6g\epsilon)
to obtain
\mathcal{S}_{i}
. (Note that we use augmented distances rather than normal distances when apply the greedy algorithm.) It was shown
[
2
]
that the
\mathsf{path\penalty\ greedy}
algorithm contains the minimum spanning tree of the input graph. Thus,
\mathcal{S}_{i}
contains
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
as a subgraph. We then set
\mathcal{E}_{i}=\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{S}_{i})\setminus\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
; this completes the construction of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
.

We now show the second claim: the stretch of
E^{\sigma}_{i}
in
H_{<L_{i}}
is
t(1+\max\{s+4g,10g\}\epsilon)
. Let
(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})
be any edge in
E^{\sigma}_{i}\setminus F^{\sigma}_{i}
. Recall that
(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})
is not in
F^{\sigma}_{i}
because (a) both
u^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}
are in the same level-
i
cluster in the construction of the cluster graph in
Lemma

10.2
, or (b)
(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})
is parallel with another edge
(u,v)
, or (c) the edge
(\varphi_{C_{u^{\prime}}},\varphi_{C_{v^{\prime}}})
corresponding to
(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})
is removed from
\mathcal{G}_{i}
in Step 2.

Case (a) does not happen since otherwise, there is a path in
H_{<L_{i}}
of length at most
gL_{i-1}\penalty\ =\penalty\ g\epsilon L_{i}\penalty\ \leq\frac{L_{i}}{1+\psi}\penalty\ \leq\penalty\ w(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})
when
\epsilon<\frac{1}{(1+\psi)g}
, contradicting that every edge is a shortest path between its endpoints.

For case (c), observe that if
t\geq 2
, then by construction,
d_{H_{<L_{i-1}}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\leq t(1+6g\epsilon)w(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})
. Otherwise (
t=1+\epsilon
), let
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
be the shortest path between
\varphi_{C_{u^{\prime}}}
and
\varphi_{C_{v^{\prime}}}
in
\mathcal{S}_{i}
. Since
\mathcal{S}_{i}
is a
t(1+6g\epsilon)
-spanner of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
, we have:

\begin{split}\omega(\mathcal{P}^{\prime})&\leq(1+\epsilon)(1+6g\epsilon)\omega(\varphi_{C_{u^{\prime}}},\varphi_{C_{v^{\prime}}})\leq(1+(12g+1)\epsilon)w(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\end{split}

(47)

Observe that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
contains at most one edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
. Let
P^{\prime}
be a realization of
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
w.r.t
u^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}
. If
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
contains no edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
, then
P^{\prime}
is a path in
H_{<L_{i-1}}
. This implies that
d_{H\leq i}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\leq(1+(12g+1)\epsilon)w(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\leq t(1+(12g+1)\epsilon)w(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})
since
t\geq 1
. Otherwise,
P^{\prime}
contains exactly one edge
(x,y)\in F^{\sigma}_{i}
. Let
Q^{\prime}
be obtained from
P^{\prime}
by replacing edge
(x,y)
by a shortest path from
x
to
y
in
H_{<L_{i}}
. Since
d_{H_{<L_{i}}}(x,y)\leq t(1+s\cdot\epsilon)w(x,y)
. Then by
Equation

47
, we have:

w(Q^{\prime})\leq t(1+s\cdot\epsilon)w(P^{\prime})\leq t(1+(2s+12g+1)\cdot\epsilon)w(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\qquad\mbox{(since $(12g+1)\epsilon\leq 1$)}

Thus, in all cases,
d_{H_{i}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\leq t(1+(2s+12g+1)\cdot\epsilon)w(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})
.

We now consider case (b); that is,
(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})
is not in
F^{\sigma}_{i}
because it is parallel with another edge
(u,v)
. Let
C_{u}
and
C_{v}
be two level-
i
clusters containing
u
and
v
, respectively. W.l.o.g, we assume that
u^{\prime}\in C_{u}
and
v^{\prime}\in C_{v}
. Since we only keep the edge of minimum weight among all parallel edges,
w(u,v)\leq w(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})
.By property (
P3
),
\mathsf{Dm}(H_{<L_{i}}[C_{u}]),\mathsf{Dm}(H_{<L_{i}}[C_{v}])\leq g\varepsilon L_{i}
.

\begin{split}d_{H_{<L_{i}}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})&\leq d_{H_{<L_{i}}}(u,v)+\mathsf{Dm}(H_{<L_{i}}[C_{u}])+\mathsf{Dm}(H_{<L_{i}}[C_{v}])\\
&\leq t(1+(2s+12g+1)\epsilon)w(u,v)+2g\varepsilon L_{i}\\
&\leq(1+(2s+16g+1)\epsilon)w(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\qquad\mbox{(since $t\geq 1$)}\penalty\ ,\end{split}

Since
w(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\geq L_{i}/(1+\psi)\geq L_{i}/2
.

\Box

To construct the set of subgraphs
\mathbb{X}
of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
, we distinguish between two cases: (a)
t=1+\epsilon
and (b)
t\geq 2
. Subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}
constructed for the case
t=1+\epsilon
have properties similar to those of subgraphs constructed in
Section

7
; the key difference is that subgraphs constructed in our work have a larger
average potential change
, which ultimately leads to an optimal dependency on
\epsilon
of the lightness. When the stretch
t\geq 2
, we show that one can construct a set of subgraphs
\mathbb{X}
of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
with much larger potential change, which reduces the dependency of the lightness on
\epsilon
by a factor
1/\epsilon
compared to the case
t=1+\epsilon
. Our construction uses
\mathsf{SSO}
as a black box. The following lemma summarizes our construction.

Lemma 10.3
.

Given
\mathsf{SSO}
, we can construct in polynomial time a set of subgraphs
\mathbb{X}
such that every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
satisfies the three properties (
P1’
)-(
P3’
) with constant
g=223
, and graph
H_{i}
such that:

d_{H_{<L_{i}}}(u,v)\leq t(1+\max\{s_{\mathsf{SSO}}(2g),6g\}\epsilon)w(u,v)\quad\forall(u,v)\in F^{\sigma}_{i}

where
F^{\sigma}_{i}
is the set of edges defined in
Lemma

10.2
. Furthermore,
w(H_{i})\leq\lambda\Delta_{i+1}+a_{i}
such that

1.

when
t\geq 2
:

\lambda=O(\chi\epsilon^{-1})
, and
A=O(\chi\epsilon^{-1})
.

2.

when
t=1+\epsilon
:

\lambda=O(\chi\epsilon^{-1}+\epsilon^{-2})
, and
A=O(\chi\epsilon^{-1}+\epsilon^{-2})
.

Here
A\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that
\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}a_{i}\leq A\cdot w(\mathrm{MST})
.

The proof of
Lemma

10.3
is deferred to
Section

11
for the case
t\geq 2
and
Section

12
for the case
t=1+\epsilon
.

Constructing
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i+1}
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{out}_{i}=\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}\setminus(\cup_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}))
be the set of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edges that are not contained in any subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i+1}^{\prime}
be the graph with vertex set
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}
and there is an edge between two nodes
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
in
\mathcal{V}_{i+1}
of there is at least one edge in
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{out}_{i}
between two nodes in the two corresponding subgraphs
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
. Note that
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i+1}^{\prime}
could have parallel edges (but no self-loop). Since
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
is a spanning tree of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
,
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i+1}^{\prime}
must be connected.
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i+1}
is then a spanning tree of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i+1}^{\prime}
.

We are now ready to prove Item (2) of
Theorem

1.10
.

Proof:
[Proof of Item (2) of
Theorem

1.10
] We apply
Lemma

6.8
to construct a light spanner
H
for
G
where each graph
H_{i}
,
i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, is constructed using
Lemma

10.3
.

When
t\geq 2
, by Item (1) of
Lemma

10.3
and
Lemma

6.8
, the lightness of
H
is
O((\frac{O(\chi\epsilon^{-1})+O(\chi\epsilon^{-1})+1}{1+\psi})\log(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+\frac{1}{\epsilon})=O_{\epsilon}(\chi\epsilon^{-1})
. When
t=1+\epsilon
, by Item (2) of
Lemma

10.3
and
Lemma

6.8
, the lightness of
H
is
O((\frac{O(\chi\epsilon^{-1})+O(\chi\epsilon^{-1})+1}{1+\psi})\log(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})=O_{\epsilon}(\chi\epsilon^{-1}+\epsilon^{-2})
.

We now bound the stretch of
H
. By
Lemma

10.3
and
Lemma

10.2
, the stretch of edges in
E^{\sigma}_{i}
in the graph
H_{<L_{i}}
is
t(1+(2s_{\mathsf{SSO}}(2g)+16g+1)\epsilon)
with
g=223
. Thus, by
Lemma

6.8
, the stretch of
H
is
t(1+(2s_{\mathsf{SSO}}(2g)+16g+1)\epsilon)=t(1+(2s_{\mathsf{SSO}}(O(1))+O(1))\epsilon)
as claimed.

\Box

11
Clustering for Stretch
t\geq 2
: Proof of
Lemma

10.3
(1)

In this section, we prove Item (1) of
Lemma

10.3
(when the stretch
t
is at least 2). The general idea is to construct a set
\mathbb{X}
of subgraphs of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
such that each subgraph in
\mathbb{X}
has a sufficiently large local potential change, and carefully choose a subset of edges of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
, with the help from
\mathsf{SSO}
, such that the total weight could be bounded by the potential change of subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}
and distances between endpoints of edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
are preserved. (By
Lemma

7.2
, it is sufficient to preserve distances between the endpoints of edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
.) In
Lemma

11.1
below, we state desirable properties of subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}
. Recall that
H_{<L_{i-1}}
is the spanner constructed for edges of
G
of weight less than
L_{i-1}
.

Lemma 11.1
.

Let
\mathcal{G}_{i}=(\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathcal{E}_{i})
be the cluster graph. We can construct in polynomial time (i) a collection
\mathbb{X}
of subgraphs of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
and its partition into two sets
\{\mathbb{X}^{+},\mathbb{X}^{-}\}
and (ii) a partition of
\mathcal{E}_{i}
into three sets
\{\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}},\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{reduce}},\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}}\}
such that:

(1)

For every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
,
\deg_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}}(\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X}))=O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
where
\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}=(\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}})
, and
\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}
. Furthermore, if
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}
, there is no edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{reduce}}
incident to a node in
\mathcal{X}
.

(2)

Let
H_{<L_{i}}^{-}
be a subgraph obtained by adding corresponding edges of
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
to
H_{<L_{i-1}}
. Then for every edge
(u,v)
that corresponds to an edge in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{redunt}}
,
d_{H_{<L_{i}}^{-}}(u,v)\leq 2d_{G}(u,v)
.

(3)

Let
\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})=\Delta(\mathcal{X})+\sum_{\mathbf{e}\in\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}\cap\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})}w(\mathbf{e})
be the
corrected potential change
of
\mathcal{X}
. Then,
\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})\geq 0
for every
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
and

\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})=\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon L_{i}).

(48)

(4)

For every edge
(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{i}
such that
\varphi_{1}\in\mathcal{X},\varphi_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
for some subgraphs
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}
, then
(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{redunt}}_{i}
, unless a
degenerate case
happens, in which
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{reduce}}_{i}=\emptyset
and
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}=O(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
.

(5)

For every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
,
\mathcal{X}
satisfies the three properties (
P1’
)-(
P3’
) with constant
g=223
. Furthermore, if
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}
, then
|\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}|=0
.

Lemma

11.1
is analogous to
Lemma

7.3
. Here we point out two major differences, which ultimately lead to the optimal dependency on
\epsilon
of the lightness. In
Lemma

7.3
, roughly
O(1/\epsilon)
edges are added to
H_{i}
per node of
\mathcal{V}_{i}
. Furthermore, each node has
\Omega(L_{i}\epsilon^{2})
average potential change. These two facts together incur a factor of
\Omega(1/\epsilon^{3})
in the lightness. Another factor of
1/\epsilon
is due to
\psi=\epsilon
for the purpose of obtaining a fast construction. The overall lightness has a factor of
1/\epsilon^{4}
dependency on
\epsilon
. Our goal is to reduce this dependency all the way down to
1/\epsilon
. By choosing
\psi=1/250
, we already eliminate one factor of
1/\epsilon
. By carefully partitioning
\mathcal{E}_{i}
into three set of edges
\{\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}},\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{reduce}},\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}}\}
, and only taking edges of
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
to
H_{i}
, we essentially reduce the number of edges we take per node in every subgraph
\mathcal{X}
from
O(1/\epsilon)
to
O(1)
(by Item (1) in
Lemma

11.1
), thereby saving another factor of
1/\epsilon
. Finally, we show that (by Item (3) in
Lemma

11.1
), each node in
\mathbb{X}^{+}
has
\Omega(L_{i}\epsilon)
average potential change, which is larger than the average potential change of nodes in
Lemma

7.3
by a factor of
1/\epsilon
. We crucially use the fact that
t\geq 2
in bounding the average potential change here. All of these ideas together reduce the dependency on
\epsilon
from
1/\epsilon^{4}
to
1/\epsilon
as desired.

Next we show to construct
H_{i}
given that we can construct a set of subgraphs
\mathbb{X}
as claimed in
Lemma

11.1
. The proof of
Lemma

11.1
is deferred to
Section

11.2
.

11.1
Constructing
H_{i}
: Proof of
Lemma

10.3
for
t\geq 2
.

In this section, we construct graph
H_{i}
as described in
Lemma

10.3
in two steps. In Step 1, we take every edge in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}
to
H_{i}
. In Step 2, we use
\mathsf{SSO}
to construct a subset of edges
F
to provide a good stretch for edges in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{reduce}}_{i}
. Note that edges in
F
may not correspond to edges in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{reduce}}_{i}
. As the implementation of
\mathsf{SSO}
depends on the input graph, this is the only place in our framework where the structure of the input graph plays an important role in the construction of the light spanner.

Constructing
H_{i}
:
We construct
H_{i}
in two steps; initially
H_{i}
contains no edges.

•

(Step 1).
We add to
H_{i}
every edge of
E^{\sigma}_{i}
corresponding to an edge in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}
.

•

(Step 2).
Let

\mathcal{J}_{i}
be a subgraph of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
induced by
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{reduce}}
. Observe that
\mathcal{J}_{i}
is a
(L_{i}/(1+\psi),\epsilon,\beta,\Upsilon=2)
-cluster graph w.r.t
H_{<L_{i-1}}
. We run
\mathsf{SSO}
on
\mathcal{J}_{i}
to obtain a set of edges
F
. We then add every edge in
F
to
H_{i}
.

Analysis.

Recall that
F^{\sigma}_{i}
is the set of edges in
E^{\sigma}_{i}
that correspond to
\mathcal{E}_{i}
.

Lemma 11.2
.

For every edge
(u,v)\in F^{\sigma}_{i}
,
d_{H_{<L_{i}}}(u,v)\leq t(1+s_{\mathsf{SSO}}(2g)\epsilon)w(u,v)
.

Proof:

By construction, edges in
F^{\sigma}_{i}
that correspond to
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
are added to
H_{i}
and hence have stretch
1
. By Item (2) of
Lemma

11.1
, edges in
F^{\sigma}_{i}
that correspond to
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}}
have stretch
2\leq t
in
H_{<L_{i}}
. Thus, it remains to focus on edges corresponding to
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{reduce}}
. Let
(\varphi_{C_{u}},\varphi_{C_{v}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{reduce}}_{i}
be the edge corresponding to an edge
(u,v\in F^{\sigma}_{i}
. Since we add all edges of
F
to
H_{i}
, by property (2) of
\mathsf{SSO}
, the stretch of edge
(u,v)
in
H_{<L_{i}}
is at most
t(1+s_{\mathsf{SSO}}(\beta)\epsilon)=t(1+s_{\mathsf{SSO}}(2g)\epsilon)
since
\beta=2g
.

\Box

Let
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}(\mathcal{X})=\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
for each
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}=\cup_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i})
be the set of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edges that are contained in subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}
. We have the following observations.

Observation 11.3
.

(1)

\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}}\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))
. Furthermore,
(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))\geq 0
.

(2)

\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}\leq w(\mathrm{MST})
.

Lemma 11.4
.

w(H_{i})\leq\lambda\Delta_{i+1}+a_{i}
for
\lambda=O(\chi\epsilon^{-1})
and
a_{i}=O(\chi\epsilon^{-1})w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i})+O(L_{i}/\epsilon)
.

Proof:
First, we consider the non-degenerate case. Note by the
construction of
H_{i}
that we do not add any edge corresponding to an edge in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{redunt}}_{i}
to
H_{i}
. Thus, we only need to consider edges in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}\cup\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{reduce}}_{i}
. Let
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{+}=\cup_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})
and
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{-}=\cup_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}}\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})
. By
11.3
, any edge in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}
incident to a node in
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{-}
is also incident to a node in
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{+}
. Let
F^{(a)}_{i}
be the set of edges added to
H_{i}
in the construction in Step
a
,
a\in\{1,2\}
.

By Item (3) of
11.3
,
\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}=\emptyset
if
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}
. By the construction in Step 1,
F^{(1)}_{i}
includes edges in
E^{\sigma}_{i}
corresponding to
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
. By Item (1) in
Lemma

11.1
, the total weight of the edges added to
H_{i}
in Step 1 is:

\begin{split}w(F^{(1)}_{i})&=\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)L_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:averagePotential-t2}}}}}{{=}}O(\frac{1}{\epsilon})\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=O(\frac{1}{\epsilon})(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i})).\end{split}

(49)

Next, we bound
w(F^{(2)}_{i})
. By Item (1) of
Lemma

11.1
, there is no edge in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{reduce}}_{i}
incident to a node in
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{-}
. Thus,
\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{J}_{i})\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{i}^{+}
. By property (1) of
\mathsf{SSO}
, it follows that

\begin{split}w(F^{(2)}_{i})&\penalty\ \leq\penalty\ \chi|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{J}_{i})|L_{i}\leq\chi|\mathcal{V}_{i}^{+}|L_{i}=\chi\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i}=O(\chi/\epsilon)(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))\penalty\ .\end{split}

(50)

By
Equations

49
and
50
, we conclude that:

\begin{split}w(H_{i})&=O(\chi/\epsilon)(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))\leq\lambda(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))\end{split}

(51)

for some
\lambda=O(\chi/\epsilon)
.

It remains to consider the degenerate case. By Item (4) of
Lemma

11.1
, we only add to
H_{i}
edges corresponding to
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}
, and there are
O(1/\epsilon)
such edges. Thus, we have:

w(H_{i})=O(\frac{L_{i}}{\epsilon})\leq\lambda\cdot(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))+O(\frac{L_{i}}{\epsilon}),

(52)

since
\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i})\geq 0
by Item (1) in
11.3
. Thus, the lemma follows from
Equations

52
and
51
.

\Box

We are now ready to prove
Lemma

10.3
for the case
t\geq 2
, which we restate below.

See
10.3

Proof:
[Proof of Item 1.] The fact that subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}
satisfy the three properties (
P1’
)-(
P3’
) with constant
g=223
follows from Item (5) of
Lemma

11.1
. The stretch in
H_{<L_{i}}
of edges in
F^{\sigma}_{i}
follows from
Lemma

11.2
.

By
Lemma

11.4
,
w(H_{i})\leq\lambda\Delta_{i+1}+a_{i}
where
\lambda=O(\chi\epsilon^{-1})
and
a_{i}=O(\chi\epsilon^{-1})w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i})+O(L_{i}/\epsilon)
. It remains to show that
A=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}a_{i}=O(\chi\epsilon^{-1})
. Observe that

\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}O(\frac{L_{i}}{\epsilon})\penalty\ =\penalty\ O(\frac{1}{\epsilon})\sum_{i=1}^{i_{\max}}\frac{L_{i_{\max}}}{\epsilon^{i_{\max}-i}}\penalty\ =\penalty\ O(\frac{L_{i_{\max}}}{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)})\penalty\ =\penalty\ O(\frac{1}{\epsilon})w(\mathrm{MST})\penalty\ ;

here
i_{\max}
is the maximum level. The last equation is due to that
\epsilon\leq 1/2
and every edge has weight at most
w(\mathrm{MST})
since the weight of every is the shortest distance between its endpoints. By Item (2) of
11.3
,
\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}\leq w(\mathrm{MST})
. Thus,
A=O(\chi/\epsilon)+O(1/\epsilon)=O(\chi/\epsilon)
as desired.

\Box

11.2
Clustering

In this section, we give a construction of the set of subgraphs
\mathbb{X}
of the cluster graph
\mathcal{G}_{i}
as claimed in
Lemma

11.1
. Our construction builds on the construction in
Section

8
. However, there are two specific goals we would like to achieve: the total degree of nodes in each subgraph
\mathcal{X}
in
\mathcal{G}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
is
O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
, and the average potential change of each node (up to some edge cases) is
\Omega(\epsilon L_{i})
(instead of
