\Omega(\epsilon^{2}L_{i})
as achieved in
Section

8
),

Our construction has 6 main steps (Steps 1-6). The first five steps are similar to the first five steps in the construction in
Section

8
. The major differences are in Step 2 and Step 4. In particular, in Step 2, we need to apply a clustering procedure of
[
50
]
to guarantee that the formed clusters have large average potential change. In Step 4, by using the fact that the stretch is at least 2, we form subgraphs in such a way that the potential change of the formed subgraphs is large. Step 6 is new in this paper. The idea is to post-process clusters formed in Steps 1-5 to form larger subgraphs that are trees, and hence, the average degree of nodes is
O(1)
. For those that are not grouped in the larger subgraphs, the total degree of the nodes in each subgraph is
O(1/\epsilon)
, which is at most the number of nodes. In this step, we also rely on the fact that the stretch
t\geq 2
.

Now we give the details of the construction. Recall that
g
is a constant defined in
property (P3)
(
g=223
in
Lemma

11.1
), and that
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
is a spanning tree of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
by Item (2) in
Definition

6.15
. We reuse the construction in
Lemma

8.1
for Step 1 which applies to the subgraph
\mathcal{K}_{i}
of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
with edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
, as described by the following lemma.

Lemma 11.5
.

Let
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}=\{\varphi_{C}\in\mathcal{V}:\varphi_{C}\mbox{ is incident to at least $\frac{2g}{\zeta\epsilon}$ edges in }\mathcal{E}_{i}\}
. Let
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathsf{high}+}
be obtained from
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}
by adding all neighbors that are connected to nodes in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}
via edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
. We can construct in polynomial time a collection of node-disjoint subgraphs
\mathbb{X}_{1}
of
\mathcal{K}_{i}=(\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathcal{E}_{i})
such that:

(1)

Each subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}
is a tree.

(2)

\cup_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}}\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})=\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}+}_{i}
.

(3)

L_{i}\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq(6+7\eta)L_{i}
, assuming that every node of
\mathcal{V}_{i}
has weight at most
\eta L_{i}
.

(4)

\mathcal{X}
contains a node in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}
and all of its neighbors in
\mathcal{K}_{i}
. In particular, this implies
|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\geq\frac{2g}{\zeta\epsilon}
.

We note
Lemma

11.5
is slightly more general than
Lemma

8.1
in that we parameterize the weights of nodes in
\mathcal{V}_{i}
by
\eta L_{i}
. Clearly, we can choose
\eta=g\epsilon\leq 1
when
\epsilon\leq 1/g
since every node in
\mathcal{V}_{i}
has a weight at most
g\epsilon L_{i}
by property
(P3’)
for level
i-1
. By parameterizing the weights, it will be more convenient for us to use the same construction again in Step 6 below.

Given a tree
T
, we say that a node
x\in T
is
T
-branching
if it has degree at least 3 in
T
. For brevity, we shall omit the prefix
T
in “
T
-branching” whenever this does not lead to confusion. Given a forest
F
, we say that
x
is
F
-branching
if it is
T
-branching for some tree
T\subseteq F
. Our construction of Step 2 uses the following lemma by
[
50
]
.

Lemma 11.6

(Lemma 6.12, full version
[
50
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a tree with vertex weights and edge weights. Let
L,\eta,\gamma,\beta
be parameters where
\eta\ll\gamma\ll 1
and
\beta\geq 1
. Suppose that for any vertex
v\in\mathcal{T}
and any edge
e\in\mathcal{T}
,
w(e)\leq w(v)\leq\eta L
and
w(v)\geq\eta L/\beta
. There is a polynomial-time algorithm that finds a collection of vertex-disjoint subtrees
\mathbb{U}=\{\mathcal{T}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{T}_{k}\}
of
\mathcal{T}
such that:

(1)

\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{T}_{i})\leq 190\gamma L
for any
1\leq i\leq k
.

(2)

Every branching node is contained in some tree in
\mathbb{U}
.

(3)

Each tree
\mathcal{T}_{i}
contains a
\mathcal{T}_{i}
-branching node
b_{i}
and three internally node-disjoint paths
\mathcal{P}_{1},\mathcal{P}_{2},\mathcal{P}_{3}
sharing
b_{i}
as the same endpoint, such that
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{P}_{1}\cup\mathcal{P}_{2})=\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{T}_{i})
and
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{P}_{3}\setminus\{b_{i}\})=\Omega(\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{T}_{i})/\beta)
. We call
b_{i}
the
center
of
\mathcal{T}_{i}
.

(4)

Let
\overline{\mathcal{T}}
be obtained by contracting each subtree of
\mathbb{U}
into a single node. Then each
\overline{\mathcal{T}}
-branching node corresponds to a sub-tree of augmented diameter at least
\gamma L
.

Figure 10:
(a) A collection
\mathbb{U}=\{\mathcal{T}_{1},\mathcal{T}_{2},\mathcal{T}_{3}\}
of a tree
\mathcal{T}
as in Lemma
11.6
. Yellow nodes are
\mathcal{T}
-branching nodes. Big yellow nodes are the centers of their corresponding subtrees in
\mathbb{U}
. (b) The shaded node in
\overline{\mathcal{T}}
is a
\overline{\mathcal{T}}
-branching node and has an augmented diameter of at least
\gamma L
.

Let
\textsc{TreeClustering}(\mathcal{T},L,\eta,\gamma,\beta)
be the output of
Lemma

11.6
for input
\mathcal{T}
and parameters
L,\eta,\gamma,\beta
.

See an illustration of Lemma
11.6
in Figure
10
. We are now ready to describe Step 2. Recall that
\zeta=1/250
is the constant in property
(P3’)

Lemma 11.7

(Step 2)
.

Let
\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}
be the forest obtained from
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
by removing every node in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}+}_{i}
(defined in
Lemma

11.5
). Let
\mathcal{U}=\cup_{\tilde{T}\in\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}}\textsc{TreeClustering}(\tilde{T},L_{i},g\epsilon,\zeta,g/\zeta)
and
\mathbb{X}_{2}=\{\tilde{T}\in\mathcal{U}:\mathsf{Adm}(\tilde{T})\geq\zeta L_{i}\}
. Then, for every
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{2}
,

(1)

\mathcal{X}
is a subtree of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
.

(2)

\zeta L_{i}\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq L_{i}
.

(3)

|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|=\Omega(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
when
\epsilon\leq 2/g
.

(4)

\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(L_{i})
.

Furthermore, let
\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
be obtained from
\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}
by removing every tree in
\mathbb{U}
that is added to
\mathbb{X}_{2}
, and contracting each remaining tree in
\mathbb{U}
into a single node. Then every tree
\overline{T}\subseteq\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
is a path.

Proof:
We observe that Items (1), (2), and (3) follows directly from the construction. We focus on showing Item (4). Let
\varphi_{b}
be the center node of
\mathcal{X}
. By Item (3) in
Lemma

11.6
, there are three internally node-disjoint paths
\mathcal{P}_{1},\mathcal{P}_{2},\mathcal{P}_{3}
sharing
\varphi_{b}
as the same endpoint. There must be an least one path, say
\mathcal{P}_{1}
, such that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\cap\mathcal{D}\subseteq\{\varphi_{b}\}
. That is,
\mathcal{P}_{1}
is internally disjoint from the diameter path
\mathcal{D}
. Also by Item (3) in
Lemma

11.6
,
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{P}_{1}\setminus\{\varphi_{b}\})=\Omega(\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})/\beta)=\Omega(\zeta L_{i}/(g/\zeta))=\Omega(L_{i})
. Thus,
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})\geq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{P}_{1}\setminus\{\varphi_{b}\})=\Omega(L_{i}),
as claimed.

\Box

By Item (4) of
Lemma

11.7
, the amount of potential change of subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}_{2}
is
\Omega(L_{i})
, while in subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}_{2}
in the construction in
Section

8
only have
\Omega(\epsilon L_{i})
potential change.

We note that there might be isolated nodes in
\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
, which we still consider as paths. We refer to nodes in
\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
that are contracted from
\mathcal{U}
as
contracted nodes
, and nodes that correspond to original nodes of
\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}
as
uncontracted nodes
. For each node
\bar{\varphi}\in\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
, we abuse notation by denoting
\bar{\varphi}
the subtree of
\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}
corresponding to the node
\bar{\varphi}
;
\bar{\varphi}
could be a single node in
\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}
for the uncontracted case. We then define the weight function of
\bar{\varphi}
as follows:

\omega(\bar{\varphi})=\mathsf{Adm}(\bar{\varphi})

(53)

In the RHS of
Equation

53
, we interpret
\bar{\varphi}
as a subtree of
\widetilde{F}^{(2)}_{i}
with weights on nodes an edges.

Observation 11.8
.

\omega(\bar{\varphi})\leq\zeta L_{i}
for every node
\bar{\varphi}\in\overline{F}^{(3)}
.

For each subpath
\overline{P}\subseteq\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
, let
\tilde{P}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}
be the subtree of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
obtained by uncontracting the contracted nodes in
\overline{P}
. We say that a node
\bar{\varphi}\in\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
is
incident to an edge

\mathbf{e}\in\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}\cup\mathcal{E}_{i}
if one endpoint of
\mathbf{e}
belongs to
\bar{\varphi}
.

Step 3: Augmenting
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}
.

Let
\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
be the forest obtained in Item (4b) in
Lemma

11.7
. Let
\bar{A}
be the set of all nodes
\bar{\varphi}
in
\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
such that there is (at least one)
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edge
\mathbf{e}=(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})
between a node
\varphi_{1}\in\bar{\varphi}
, and a node
\varphi_{2}\in\mathcal{X}
for some subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}
. Then, for each node
\bar{\varphi}\in\bar{A}
, we augment
\mathcal{X}
by adding
\bar{\varphi}
and
\mathbf{e}
to
\mathcal{X}
.

The following lemma follows directly from the construction.

Lemma 11.9
.

The augmentation in Step 3 increases the augmented diameter of each subgraph in
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}
by at most
4L_{i}
when
\epsilon\leq 1/g
.

Furthermore, let
\overline{F}^{(4)}_{i}
be the forest obtained from
\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
by removing every node in
\bar{A}
. Then, for every path
\overline{P}\subseteq\overline{F}^{(4)}_{i}
, at least one endpoint
\bar{\varphi}\in\overline{P}
has an
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edge to a subgraph of
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}
, unless
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}=\emptyset
.

Required definitions/preparations for Step 4.

Let
\overline{F}^{(4)}_{i}
be the forest obtained from
\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
as described in
Lemma

11.9
. We call every path of augmented diameter at least
6L_{i}
of
\overline{F}^{(4)}_{i}
a
long path
. We use red/blue coloring, which is analogous to
red/blue coloring
in
Section

8
.

Red/Blue Coloring.
Given a path
\overline{P}\subseteq\overline{F}^{(4)}_{i}
, we color their nodes red or blue. If a node has augmented distance at most
L_{i}
from at least one of the path’s endpoints, we color it red; otherwise, we color it blue. Observe that each red node belongs to the suffix or prefix of
\overline{P}
; the other nodes are colored blue.

For each blue node
\bar{\nu}
in a long path
\overline{P}
, we denote by
\overline{I}(\bar{\nu})
the subpath of
\overline{P}
containing every node within an augmented distance (in
\overline{P}
) at most
(1-\psi)L_{i}
from
\bar{\nu}
. We call
\overline{I}(\bar{\nu})
the
interval
of
\bar{\nu}
. Recall that
\psi=1/250
is the constant defined in
Equation

22
.

We define the following set of edges between nodes of
\overline{F}^{(4)}_{i}
.

\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i}=\{(\bar{\mu},\bar{\nu})|\exists\mu\in\bar{\mu},\nu\in\bar{\nu}\mbox{ and }(\mu,\nu)\in\mathcal{E}_{i}\}.

(54)

We note that there is no edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
whose nodes belong to the same tree, say
\bar{\mu}
, that corresponds to a node in
\overline{F}^{(4)}_{i}
, because such an edge, say
\mathbf{e}
, will have weight at most
\omega(\bar{\mu})\leq\zeta L_{i}<L_{i}/2<\omega(\mathbf{e})
, a contradiction.

Next, we define the weight:

\omega(\bar{\mu},\bar{\nu})=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\in\bar{\mu},\nu\in\bar{\nu}\\
(\mu,\nu)\in\mathcal{E}_{i}\end{subarray}}\omega(\mu,\nu)

(55)

That is, the weight of edges
(\bar{\mu},\bar{\nu})
is the minimum weight over all edges between two trees
\bar{\mu}
and
\bar{\nu}
. We then denote
(\mu,\nu)
the edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
corresponding to an edge
(\bar{\mu},\bar{\nu})\in\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
. Next, we define:

\begin{split}\bar{\mathcal{E}}^{far}_{i}(\overline{F}^{(4)})&=\{(\bar{\nu},\bar{\mu})\in\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i}|color(\bar{\nu})=color({\bar{\mu}})=blue\mbox{ and }\overline{I}(\bar{\nu})\cap\overline{I}(\bar{\mu})=\emptyset\}\\
\bar{\mathcal{E}}^{close}_{i}(\overline{F}^{(4)})&=\{(\bar{\nu},\bar{\mu})\in\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i}|color(\bar{\nu})=color({\bar{\mu}})=blue\mbox{ and }\overline{I}(\bar{\nu})\cap\overline{I}(\bar{\mu})\not=\emptyset\}\end{split}

(56)

We note that the definition of
\bar{\mathcal{E}}^{far}_{i}(\overline{F}^{(4)})
and
\bar{\mathcal{E}}^{close}_{i}(\overline{F}^{(4)})
depends on the underlying forest
\overline{F}^{(4)}
.

Lemma 11.10

(Step 4)
.

Let
\overline{F}^{(4)}_{i}
be the forest obtained from
\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
as described in
Lemma

11.9
. We can construct a collection
\mathbb{X}_{4}
of subgraphs of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
such that every
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{4}
:

(1)

\mathcal{X}
is a tree and contains a single edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
.

(2)

L_{i}\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq 5L_{i}
.

(3)

|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|=\Omega(1/\epsilon)
when
\epsilon\leq 1/8
.

(4)

\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(L_{i})
.

Let
\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}
be obtained from
\overline{F}^{(4)}_{i}
by removing every node whose corresponding tree is contained in subgraphs of
\mathbb{X}_{4}
. If we apply
Red/Blue Coloring
to each path of augmented diameter at least
6L_{i}
in
\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}
, then
\bar{\mathcal{E}}^{far}_{i}(\overline{F}^{(5)})=\emptyset
. Furthermore, for every path
\overline{P}\subseteq\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}
, at least one endpoint of
\overline{P}
has an
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edge to a subgraph of
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}
, unless
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}=\emptyset
.

Proof:
We only apply the construction to long paths of
\overline{F}^{(4)}_{i}
; those that have augmented diameter at least
6L_{i}
. We use the following claim which is analogous to
8.5
.

Claim 11.11
.

For any blue node
\nu
, it holds that

(a)

(2-3\zeta-2\epsilon-2\psi)L_{i}\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\overline{\mathcal{I}}(\bar{\nu}))\leq 2(1-\psi)L_{i}
.

(b)

Denote by
\overline{\mathcal{I}}_{1}
and
\overline{\mathcal{I}}_{2}
the two subpaths obtained by removing
\bar{\nu}
from the path
\overline{\mathcal{I}}(\bar{\nu})
.
Each of these subpaths has augmented diameter at least
(1-2\zeta-\epsilon-\psi)L_{i}
.

We now construct
\mathbb{X}_{4}
, which initially is empty.

•

Pick an edge
(\bar{\nu},\bar{\mu})
with both blue endpoints and form a subgraph
\overline{\mathcal{X}}=\{(\bar{\nu},\bar{\mu})\cup\overline{\mathcal{I}}(\bar{\nu})\cup\overline{\mathcal{I}}(\bar{\mu})\}
. We remove all nodes in
\overline{\mathcal{I}}(\bar{\nu})\cup\overline{\mathcal{I}}(\bar{\mu})
from the path or two paths containing
\bar{\nu}
and
\bar{\mu}
, update the color of nodes in the new paths to satisfy
Red/Blue Coloring
. We then uncontract nodes in
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
to obtain a subgraph
\mathcal{X}
of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
, add
\mathcal{X}
to
\mathbb{X}_{4}
, and repeat this step until it no longer applies.

Items (1), (2) and (3) follows from the same argument in
Lemma

8.4
. We only focus on Item (4). Let
\overline{\mathcal{I}}_{1},\overline{\mathcal{I}}_{2},\overline{\mathcal{I}}_{3},\overline{\mathcal{I}}_{4}
be four paths obtained from
\overline{\mathcal{I}}(\bar{\mu})
and
\overline{\mathcal{I}}(\bar{\nu})
by removing
\bar{\mu}
and
\bar{\nu}
. Let
\overline{\mathcal{D}}
be the diameter path of
\bar{\mathcal{X}}
. Then
\overline{\mathcal{D}}
contains at most 2 paths among the four paths, and possibly contains edge
(\bar{\nu},\bar{\mu})
as well. Since each path has augmented diameter at most
2L_{i}
and
\omega(\bar{\nu},\bar{\mu})\leq L_{i}
, we have that:

\begin{split}Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})\geq\left(\sum_{\bar{\varphi}\in\bar{\mathcal{X}}}\omega(\bar{\varphi})+\sum_{\mathbf{e}\in\mathcal{E}(\bar{\mathcal{X}})\cap\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}}\omega(\mathbf{e})\right)-\mathsf{Adm}(\bar{\mathcal{D}})\geq(1-8\zeta-4\epsilon-4\psi)L_{i}=\Omega(L_{i})\penalty\ ,\end{split}

when
\epsilon\leq 1/8
.

\Box

As each node has weight at most
g\epsilon L_{i-1}
, we have:

Observation 11.12
.

Let
\overline{P}\subseteq\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
be a path of augmented diameter
\Omega(L_{i})
. Then
|\mathcal{V}(\tilde{P}^{\mathsf{uctrt}})|=\Omega(1/\epsilon)
.

Step 5.

Let
\overline{P}
be a path in
\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}
obtained by Item (5) of
Lemma

11.10
. We construct two sets of subgraphs, denoted by
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}_{5}
and
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{pref}}_{5}
, of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
. The construction is broken into two steps. Step 5A is only applicable when
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}\not=\emptyset
.

•

(Step 5A)
If
\overline{P}
has augmented diameter at most
6L_{i}
, let
\mathbf{e}
be an
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edge connecting
\widetilde{P}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}
and a node in some subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}
;
\mathbf{e}
exists by

11.10
. We add both
\mathbf{e}
and
\widetilde{P}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}
to
\mathcal{X}
.

•

(Step 5B)
Otherwise, the augmented diameter of
\overline{P}
is at least
6L_{i}
. In this case, we greedily break
\overline{P}
into subpaths
\{\overline{Q}_{1},\ldots,\overline{Q}_{k}\}
such that for each

,
\tilde{Q}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}_{j}
has augmented diameter at least
L_{i}
and at most
2L_{i}
. If
\overline{Q}_{j}

via an edge

, we add
\tilde{Q}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}_{j}
and

to
\mathcal{X}
. If
\overline{Q}_{j}
contains an endpoint of
\overline{P}
, we add
\widetilde{Q}_{j}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}
to
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{pref}}_{5}
; otherwise, we add
\widetilde{Q}_{j}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}
to
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}_{5}
.

In Step 5B, we want
\tilde{Q}_{j}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}
to have augmented diameter at least
L_{i}
(to satisfy property
(P3’)
) instead of requiring
\mathsf{Adm}(\overline{Q}_{j})\geq L_{i}
because a lower bound on the augmented diameter of
\overline{Q}_{j}
does not translate to a lower bound on the augmented diameter of
\tilde{Q}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}_{j}
.

Lemma 11.13
.

Every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}\cup\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
satisfies:

(1)

\mathcal{X}
is a subtree of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
.

(2)

L_{i}\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq 2L_{i}
.

(3)

|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|=\Omega(1/\epsilon)
.

Furthermore, if
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
, then
\mathcal{X}
the uncontraction of a prefix subpath
\overline{Q}
of a long path
\overline{P}
, and additionally, the (uncontraction of) other suffix
\overline{Q}^{\prime}
of

is augmented to a subgraph in

, unless

Proof:

Items (1) and (2) follow directly from the construction. Item (3) follows from
11.12
. The last claim about subgraphs in

.

\Box

Lemma 11.14
.

Let
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}=\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}\cup\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}\cup\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
. Every node of
\mathcal{V}_{i}
is grouped to some subgraph in
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
. Furthermore, for every
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
,

(1)

\mathcal{X}
is a tree. Furthermore, if
\mathcal{X}\not\in\mathbb{X}_{4}
, it is a subtree of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
.

(2)

\zeta L_{i}\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq 31L_{i}
when
\epsilon\leq 1/g
.

(3)

|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|=\Omega(1/\epsilon)
.

Proof:

The fact that every node of
\mathcal{V}_{i}
is grouped to some subgraph in
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
follows directly from the construction. Observe that only subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
formed in Step 4 contain edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
, and such subgraphs are trees by Item (1) of
Lemma

11.10
; this implies Item (1). Item 3 follows directly from
Lemmas

11.5
,
11.7
,
11.10
and
11.13
.

We now focus on bounding
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})
. The lower bound on
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})
follows directly from Item (3) of
Lemma

11.5
, Items (2) of
Lemmas

11.7
,
11.10
and
11.13
. For the upper bound, we observe that if
\mathcal{X}
is formed in Step 1, it could be augmented further in Step 3, and hence, by Item (3) of
Lemma

11.5
(here
\eta=g\epsilon
), and
Lemma

11.9
,
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq(6+7g\epsilon)L_{i}+4L_{i}\leq 17L_{i}
since
\epsilon\leq 1/g
. By Items (2) of
Lemmas

11.7
,
11.10
and
11.13
,
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq 5L_{i}
if
\mathcal{X}
is not initially formed in Step 1. Furthermore, the augmentation in Step 5A and 5B increases
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})
by at most
2(\bar{w}+6L_{i})\leq 14L_{i}
. This implies that, in any case,
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq\max\{17L_{i},5L_{i}\}+14L_{i}=31L_{i}
.

\Box

Except for subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}\cup\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
, we can show every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}
has large potential change:
\Delta_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(L_{i})
. The last property that we need to complete the proof of
Lemma

11.1
is to guarantee that the total degree of vertices in
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}\cup\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}\cup\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
in
\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{reduce}}
is
O(1/\epsilon)
(we have not defined
\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{reduce}}
yet). To this end, we need Step 6. The basic idea is that if any subgraph has many out-going edges in
\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
(defined in
Equation

54
), then we apply the clustering procedure in Step 1 to group it to a larger subgraph.

Required definitions/preparations for Step 6.

We construct a graph
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{i},\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{i},\widehat{\omega})
as follows. Each node
\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{X}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{i}}
corresponds to a subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
. We then set
\widehat{\omega}(\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{X}})=\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})
. There is an edge
(\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{X}},\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{Y}})\in\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
between two
different nodes

\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{X}},\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{Y}}
if there exists an edge
(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{i}
between a node
\varphi_{1}\in\mathcal{X}
and a node
\varphi_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
. We set the weight
\widehat{\omega}(\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{X}},\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{Y}})
to be the minimum weight over all edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
between
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
. We call nodes of
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{i}

supernodes
.

We call
\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{X}}
a
heavy
supernode if
|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\geq\frac{2g}{\zeta\epsilon}
or
\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{X}}
is incident to at least
\frac{2g}{\zeta\epsilon}
edges in
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{i}
. Otherwise, we call
\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{X}}
a
light
supernode. By definition of a heavy supernode and by Item (4) in
Lemma

11.5
, if
\mathcal{X}
is formed in Step 1, then
\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{X}}
is a heavy supernode. We then do the following.

We apply the construction in
Lemma

11.5
to graph
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{i},\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{i},\widehat{\omega})
, where
\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{i}^{\mathsf{high}}
is the set of heavy supernodes in
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{i}^{\mathsf{high}^{+}}
is obtained from
\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{i}^{\mathsf{high}}
by adding neighbors in
\widehat{\mathcal{K}_{i}}
. Let
\widehat{\mathbb{X}}_{6}
be the set of subgraphs of
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{i},\widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{i},\widehat{\omega})
obtained by the construction. Every subgraph
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}\in\widehat{\mathbb{X}}_{6}
satisfies all properties in
Lemma

11.5
with
\eta=31
.

Let
\mathbb{X}_{6}
be obtained from
\widehat{\mathbb{X}}_{6}
by uncontracting supernodes. This completes our Step 6.

By the construction and a simple calculation, we have:

Lemma 11.15
.

Every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{6}
has
\zeta L_{i}\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq 223L_{i}
.

In
Section

11.2.1
we construct the set of subgraphs
\mathbb{X}
, and show several properties of subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}
. In
Section

11.2.2
, we construct a partition of
\mathcal{E}_{i}
into three sets, and prove
Lemma

11.1
.

11.2.1
Constructing
\mathbb{X}

For each
i\in\{2,4,5\}
let
\mathbb{X}_{i}^{-}
be obtained from
\mathbb{X}_{i}
by removing subgraphs corresponding to nodes in
\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{i}^{\mathsf{high}^{+}}
(which then form subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}_{6}
). We now define
\mathbb{X}
and a partition of
\mathbb{X}
into two sets
\mathbb{X}^{+}
and
\mathbb{X}^{-}

\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{low}^{-}}
as claimed in
Lemma

11.1
. We distinguish two cases:

Degenerate Case.

The degenerate case is the case where
\mathbb{X}^{-}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}^{-}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}^{-}_{4}=\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}_{5}=\emptyset
. In this case, we set
\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{X}^{-}=\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}\cup\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
, and
\mathbb{X}^{+}=\emptyset
.

Non-degenerate case.

If
\mathbb{X}^{-}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}^{-}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}^{-}_{4}=\mathbb{X}_{6}\not=\emptyset
, we call this the non-degenerate case. In this case, we define.

\begin{split}\mathbb{X}^{+}&=\mathbb{X}^{-}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}^{-}_{4}\cup\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}-}\cup\mathbb{X}_{6},\quad\mathbb{X}^{-}=\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{intrnl}-}\\
\mathbb{X}&=\mathbb{X}^{+}\cup\mathbb{X}^{-}\end{split}

(57)

We note that every subgraph in
\mathbb{X}_{1}
corresponds to a heavy supernode in
\widehat{\mathcal{K}_{i}}
and hence, it will be grouped in some subgraph in
\mathbb{X}_{6}
.

In the analysis below, we only explicitly distinguish the degenerate case from the non-degenerate case when it is necessary, i.e, in the proof Item (4) of
Lemma

11.1
. Otherwise, which case we are in is either implicit from the context, or does not matter.

Lemma 11.16
.

Let
\mathbb{X}
be the subgraph as defined in
Equation

57
. For every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
,
\mathcal{X}
is a tree and satisfies the three properties (
P1’
)-(
P3’
) with
g=223
. Consequently, Item (5) of
Lemma

11.1
holds.

Proof:
We observe that property
(P1’)
follows directly from the construction. Property
(P2’)
follows from Item (3) of
Lemma

11.14
. Property
(P3’)
follows from
Lemma

11.15
.

By Item (1) of
Lemma

11.14
, every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
is a tree. Since subgraphs in
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{6}
in the construction of Step 6 are trees, subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}
are also trees. Thus,
|\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}|=O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
. Furthermore, if
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}
, by the definition
\mathbb{X}^{-}
,
\mathcal{X}\not\in\mathbb{X}_{4}
. Thus,
\mathcal{X}
is a subtree of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
by Item (1) of
Lemma

11.14
. That implies
\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}=\emptyset
, which implies Item (5) of
Lemma

11.1
.

\Box

Our next goal is to show Item (3) of
Lemma

11.1
.
Lemma

11.17
below implies that if
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
is formed in Steps 2,4, and 6, then
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(\epsilon L_{i}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
.

Lemma 11.17
.

For any subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
such that
|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\geq\frac{2g}{\zeta\epsilon}
or
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(L_{i})
, then
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(\epsilon L_{i}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
.

Proof:
We fist consider the case where
|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\geq\frac{2g}{\zeta\epsilon}
. By definition of corrected potential change in Item (3) of
Lemma

11.1
, we have:

\begin{split}\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})&\geq\sum_{\varphi\in\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})}\omega(\varphi)-\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\geq(\zeta\epsilon L_{i}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)-\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\\
&\geq(\zeta\epsilon L_{i}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)/2-gL_{i}+(\zeta\epsilon L_{i}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)/2=\Omega(\epsilon L_{i}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)\penalty\ .\end{split}

Next, we consider the case where
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(L_{i})
. If
|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\geq\frac{2g}{\zeta\epsilon}
, then
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(\epsilon L_{i}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
as we have just shown. Otherwise, we have:

\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(L_{i})=\Omega(\epsilon L_{i}\frac{2g}{\zeta\epsilon})=\Omega(\epsilon L_{i}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|),

as claimed.

\Box

Lemma 11.18
.

\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})\geq 0
for every
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
and

\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})=\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon L_{i}).

Consequently, Item (3) of
Lemma

11.1
holds.

Proof:
The fact that
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})\geq 0
follows directly from the definition. By
Lemma

11.17
for every
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}^{-}_{4}\cup\mathbb{X}_{6}
, it holds that

\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(\epsilon L_{i}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)\penalty\ ,

(58)

By the definition of
\mathbb{X}^{+}
in
Equation

57
, the only case where
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})
could be
0
is
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}-}
. Next, we use an averaging argument to assign potential change to
\mathcal{X}
. Observe that
\mathcal{X}
is an uncontraction of some prefix
\overline{Q}
of some path
\overline{P}\in\overline{F}^{(5)}
. By
Lemma

11.13
, the uncontraction of the other suffix
\overline{Q}^{\prime}
of
\overline{P}
, say
\widetilde{Q}^{\prime}
, is augmented to a subgraph in
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}
. It follows that
\widetilde{Q}^{\prime}
is a subgraph of some graph
\mathcal{Y}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}^{-}_{4}\cup\mathbb{X}_{6}
. If we distribute the corrected potential change
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{Y})
to nodes in
\mathcal{Y}
, each node gets
\Omega(\epsilon L_{i})
potential change. Thus, the total potential change of nodes in
\widetilde{Q}^{\prime}
is
\Omega(\epsilon L_{i}|\mathcal{V}(\widetilde{Q}^{\prime})|)
. By Item (3) of
Lemma

11.13
,
|\mathcal{V}(\widetilde{Q}^{\prime})|=\Omega(1/\epsilon)
. Thus the potential change of nodes in
\widetilde{Q}^{\prime}
is
\Omega(L_{i}|)
. We distribute
half
of the potential change to
\mathcal{X}
. Thus,
\mathcal{X}
has
\Omega(L_{i})
potential change, and by
Lemma

11.17
, the potential change of
\mathcal{X}
is
\Omega(\epsilon L_{i}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
. This, with
Equation

58
, implies that:

\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})=\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon L_{i}),

as desired.

\Box

11.2.2
Constructing the partition of of
\mathcal{E}_{i}
: Proof of
Lemma

11.1

In this section, we construct a partition of
\mathcal{E}
and prove
Lemma

11.1
. Items (3) and (5) of
Lemma

11.1
were proved in
Lemma

11.18
and
Lemma

11.16
, respectively. In the following, we prove Items (1), (2) and (4). Indeed, Item (2) follows directly from the construction (
11.20
). Item (1) is proved in
Lemma

11.26
and Item (4) is proved in
Lemma

11.27
and
Lemma

11.28
.

Recall that we define
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}=\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}\cup\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{pref}}_{5}\cup\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}_{5}
in
Lemma

11.14
. We say that a subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
is
light
if it corresponds to a light supernode in
\widehat{\mathcal{K}_{i}}
(defined in Step 6); otherwise, we say that
\mathcal{X}
is
heavy
. We construct
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{redunt}}_{i}
in two steps below;
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{reduce}}=\mathcal{E}_{i}\setminus(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}\cup\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}})
. Initially, both sets are empty.

Constructing
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{redunt}}_{i}
:
Let
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{light}}
be the set of light subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
.

•

Step 1:
For each subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
, we add all edges of
\mathcal{E}_{i}
in
\mathcal{X}
to
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
. That is,

\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}\leftarrow\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}\cup(\mathcal{E}_{i}\cap\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})).

•

Step 2:
We construct a graph
\mathcal{H}_{i}=(\mathcal{V}_{i},\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}\cup\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}},\omega)
. We then consider every edge
\mathbf{e}=(\nu\cup\mu)\in\mathcal{E}_{i}
, where both endpoints are in subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{light}}
, in the non-decreasing order of the weight. If:

d_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}(\nu,\mu)>2\omega(\mathbf{e})\penalty\ ,

(59)

then we add
\mathbf{e}
to
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
(and hence, also to
\mathcal{H}_{i}
). Otherwise, we add
\mathbf{e}
to
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}}
. Note that the distance in
\mathcal{H}_{i}
in
Equation

59
is the augmented distance.

The construction in Step 2 is the
\mathsf{path\penalty\ greedy}
algorithm. We observe that:

Observation 11.19
.

For every edge
\mathbf{e}\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{reduce}}_{i}
, at least one endpoint of
\mathbf{e}
is in a heavy subgraph.

Observation 11.20
.

Let
H_{<L_{i}}^{-}
be a subgraph obtained by adding corresponding edges of
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
to
H_{<L_{i-1}}
. Then for every edge
(u,v)
that corresponds to an edge in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{redunt}}
,
d_{H_{<L_{i}}^{-}}(u,v)\leq 2d_{G}(u,v)
.

We now focus on proving Item (1) of
Lemma

11.1
. The key idea is the following lemma.

Lemma 11.21
.

Any subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\setminus\mathbb{X}_{1}
can be partitioned into
k=O(1/\zeta)
subgraphs
\{\mathcal{Y}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{Y}_{k}\}
such that
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{Y}_{j})\leq 9\zeta L_{i}
for any
1\leq j\leq k
when
\epsilon\leq\frac{\zeta}{g}
.

Proof:

Let
\varphi
be a branching node in
\widetilde{F}^{(2)}
, the tree in
Lemma

11.7
. We say that
\varphi
is
special
if there exists three internally node disjoint paths
\widetilde{P}_{1},\widetilde{P}_{2},\widetilde{P}_{3}
of
\widetilde{F}^{(2)}
sharing the same node
\varphi
such that
\mathsf{Adm}(\widetilde{P}_{j}\setminus\{\varphi\})\geq\zeta L_{i}
. Observe by the construction in
Lemma

11.7
that

Observation 11.22
.

Any special node
\varphi
of
\widetilde{F}^{(2)}
is contained in a subgraph in
\mathbb{X}_{2}
.

By
Lemma

11.14
,
\mathcal{X}
is a tree. Let
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
be a maximal subtree of
\mathcal{X}
such that
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
is a subtree of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
. If
\mathcal{X}
is in
\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}\cup\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}
then
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=\mathcal{X}
. Otherwise,
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{4}
, and thus it has a single edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
by Item (1) of
Lemma

11.10
. That is,
\mathcal{X}
has exactly two such maximal subtrees
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
. Thus, to complete the lemma, we show that
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
can be partitioned into
O(1/\zeta)
subtrees as claimed in the lemma.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be the path in
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
of maximum augmented diameter. Let
\mathcal{J}
be the forest obtained from
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
by removing nodes of
\mathcal{D}
. Then,

Observation 11.23
.

\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{T})\leq 2\zeta L_{i}\quad\forall\mbox{ tree }\mathcal{T}\in\mathcal{J}

Now we greedily partition
\mathcal{D}
into
k=O(1/\zeta)
subpaths
\{\mathcal{P}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{P}_{k}\}
, each of augmented diameter at least
\zeta L_{i}
and at most
3\zeta L_{i}
. This is possible because each node/edge has a weight at most
\max\{g\epsilon L_{i},\bar{w}\}\leq\max\{g\epsilon L_{i},\epsilon L_{i}\}\leq\zeta L_{i}
when
\epsilon\leq\zeta/g
. Next, for every tree
\mathcal{T}\in\mathcal{J}
, if
\mathcal{T}
is connected to a node
\varphi\in\mathcal{P}_{j}
via some
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edge
\mathbf{e}
for some
j\in[1,k]
, we augment
\mathbf{e}
and
\mathcal{T}
to
\mathcal{P}_{j}
. By
11.23
, the augmentation increases the diameter of
\mathcal{P}
by at most
2(\bar{w}+2\zeta L_{i})\leq 6\zeta L_{i}
additively.

\Box

Lemma 11.24
.

Let
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}
be two (not necessarily distinct) subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{light}}
. Then there are
O(1)
edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
between nodes in
\mathcal{X}
and nodes in
\mathcal{Y}
.

Proof:
Let
\{\mathcal{A}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{A}_{x}\}
(
\{\mathcal{B}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{B}_{y}\}
) be a partition of
\mathcal{X}
(
\mathcal{Y}
) into
x=O(1/\zeta)
(
y=O(1/\zeta)
) subgraphs of augmented diameter at most
9\zeta L_{i}
as guarantee by
Lemma

11.21
. Observe that by
Equation

59
, there is at most one edge in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}
between
\mathcal{A}_{j}
and
\mathcal{B}_{k}
for any
1\leq j\leq x,1\leq k\leq y
. Thus, the number of edges in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}
between
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
is at most
x\cdot y=O(1/\zeta^{2})=O(1)
.

\Box

We obtain the following corollary of
Lemma

11.24
.

Corollary 11.25
.

For any
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{light}}
,
\deg_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}}(\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X}))=O(1/\epsilon)=O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
where
\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}=(\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}})
.

We now prove Item (1) of
Lemma

11.1
.

Lemma 11.26
.

For every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
,
\deg_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}}(\mathcal{X})=O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
where
\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}=(\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}})
, and
\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}
. Furthermore, if
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}
, there is no edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{reduce}}
incident to a node in
\mathcal{X}
.

Proof:
Let
\mathcal{X}
be a subgraph in
\mathbb{X}
. Observe by the construction of
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}
in Step 1,
\mathcal{E}\cap\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}
. Clearly, the number of edges incident to nodes in
\mathcal{X}
added in Step 1 is
O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
since every subgraph in
\mathbb{X}
is a tree by
Lemma

11.14
. Thus, it remains to bound the number of edges added in Step 2.

If
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}^{-}_{4}\cup\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{pref}-}_{5}\cup\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{intrnl}-}_{5}
, then
\mathcal{X}
corresponds to a light supernode in
\mathcal{K}_{i}
. Thus,
\deg_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}}(\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X}))=O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
by
Corollary

11.25
. Otherwise,
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{6}
. By construction in Step 6,
\mathcal{X}
is the union heavy subgraphs and light subgraphs (and some edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
). By construction of
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
, only light subgraphs have nodes incident to edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
. Let
\{\mathcal{Y}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{Y}_{p}\}
be the set of light subgraphs constituting
\mathcal{X}
. Then, by
Corollary

11.25
, we have that:

\deg_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}}(\mathcal{X})\leq\sum_{k=1}^{p}\deg_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}}(\mathcal{Y})=\sum_{k=1}^{p}(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{Y}_{k})|)=O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)\penalty\ .

We now show that there is no edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{reduce}}
incident to a node in
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}
. Suppose otherwise, let
\mathbf{e}
be such an edge.
By
11.19
,
\mathbf{e}
is incident to a node in a heavy subgraph, say
\mathcal{Y}
. That is,
\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{Y}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathsf{high}}_{i}
. By the construction in Step 6,
\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathcal{X}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathsf{high}^{+}}_{i}
and hence
\mathcal{X}
is grouped to a larger subgraph in
\mathbb{X}_{6}
, contradicting that
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}
.

\Box

We now focus on proving Item (4) of
Lemma

11.1
. In
Lemma

11.27
, we consider the non-degenerate case, and in
Lemma

11.28
we consider the degenerate case.

Lemma 11.27
.

Let
(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})
be any edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
between nodes of two light subgraphs
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}
in
\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}
. Then,
(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}}
.

Proof:
By the construction of Step 5,
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
correspond to two subpaths
\bar{{\mathcal{X}}}
and
\bar{\mathcal{Y}}
of two paths
\overline{P}
and
\overline{Q}
in
\overline{F}^{(5)}
. Note that all nodes in
\bar{{\mathcal{X}}}
and
\bar{\mathcal{Y}}
have a blue color since the suffix/prefix of
\overline{P}
and
\overline{Q}
are either in
\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
or are augmented to existing subgraphs in Step 5B.

Since there is an edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
between
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
, there must be an edge in
\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
, say
(\bar{\mu},\bar{\nu})
between a node of
\bar{\mu}\in\bar{{\mathcal{X}}}
and a node of
\bar{\nu}\in\bar{\mathcal{Y}}
by the definition of
\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
(in
Equation

54
) such that
\varphi_{1}\in\bar{\mu},\varphi_{2}\in\bar{\nu}
.

As
\bar{\mu}
and
\bar{\nu}
both have a blue color, either
(\bar{\mu},\bar{\nu})\in\mathcal{E}_{i}^{far}(\overline{F}^{(5)})
or
(\bar{\mu},\bar{\nu})\in\mathcal{E}_{i}^{close}(\overline{F}^{(5)})
by the definition in
Equation

56
. By
Lemma

11.10
,
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{far}(\overline{F}^{(5)})=\emptyset
. Thus,
(\bar{\mu},\bar{\nu})\in\mathcal{E}_{i}^{close}(\overline{F}^{(5)})
. This implies
\overline{I}(\bar{\nu})\cap\overline{I}(\bar{\mu})\not=\emptyset
, and hence,
\bar{{\mathcal{X}}}
and
\bar{\mathcal{Y}}
are broken from the same path, say
\bar{P}\in\overline{F}^{(5)}
, in Step 5B.

Furthermore, by the definition of
\overline{I}(\bar{\nu})
, every node
\bar{\varphi}\in\overline{I}(\bar{\nu})
is within an augmented distance (along
\overline{P}
) of at most
(1-\psi)L_{i}
from
\bar{\nu}
. This means,
\mathsf{Adm}(\bar{P}[\bar{\nu},\bar{\mu}])\leq 2(1-\psi)L_{i}
. Note that the uncontraction of
\bar{P}[\bar{\nu},\bar{\mu}]
is a subtree of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
. Thus,
d_{\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}}(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\bar{P}[\bar{\nu},\bar{\mu}])\leq 2(1-\psi)L_{i}\leq\frac{2L_{i}}{1+\psi}\leq 2\omega(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})
. As
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
is a subgraph of
\mathcal{H}_{i}
,
(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})
will be added to
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}}
in Step 2,
Equation

59
.

\Box

Lemma 11.28

(Structure of Degenerate Case)
.

If the degenerate case happens, then

\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}=\overline{F}^{(4)}_{i}=\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
, and
\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}
is a single (long) path. Moreover,
|\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}|=O(1/\epsilon)
.

Proof:
Recall that the degenerate case happens when
\mathbb{X}^{-}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}^{-}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}^{-}_{4}=\mathbb{X}_{6}=\emptyset
. This implies
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}=\emptyset
. Thus,
\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}=\overline{F}^{(4)}_{i}=\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
. Furthermore,
\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}
is a single (long) path since
\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
is a path by
Lemma

11.7
. This gives
|\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}|=2
. By
Lemma

11.27
, there is no edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
between two subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}
. Thus, any edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
must be incident to a node in a subgraph of
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
. By
Corollary

11.25
, there are
O(1/\epsilon)
such edges.

\Box

12
Clustering for Stretch
t=1+\epsilon

In this section, we prove
Lemma

10.3
when the stretch
t=1+\epsilon
. The key technical idea is the following clustering lemma, which is analogous to
Lemma

11.1
in
Section

11
; the highlighted texts below are the major differences. Recall that
H_{<L_{i-1}}
is the spanner constructed for edges of
G
of weight less than
L_{i-1}
.

Lemma 12.1
.

Let
\mathcal{G}_{i}=(\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathcal{E}_{i})
be the cluster graph. We can construct in polynomial time (i) a collection
\mathbb{X}
of subgraphs of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
and its partition into two sets
\{\mathbb{X}^{+},\mathbb{X}^{-}\}
and (ii) a partition of
\mathcal{E}_{i}
into three sets
\{\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}},\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{reduce}},\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}}\}
such that:

(1)

For every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
,
\deg_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}}(\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X}))=O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|/\epsilon)
where
\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}=(\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}})
, and
\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}
. Furthermore, if
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}
, there is no edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{reduce}}
incident to a node in
\mathcal{X}
.

(2)

Let
H_{<L_{i}}^{-}
be a subgraph obtained by adding corresponding edges of
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
to
H_{<L_{i-1}}
. Then for every edge
(u,v)
that corresponds to an edge in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{redunt}}
,
d_{H_{<L_{i}}^{-}}(u,v)\leq(1+6g\epsilon)2d_{G}(u,v)
.

(3)

Let
\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})=\Delta(\mathcal{X})+\sum_{\mathbf{e}\in\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}\cap\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})}w(\mathbf{e})
be the
corrected potential change
of
\mathcal{X}
. Then,
\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})\geq 0
for every
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
and

\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})=\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon L_{i}).

(60)

(4)

There exists an orientation of edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
such that for every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}
, if
\mathcal{X}
has
t
out-going edges for some
t\geq 0
, then
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|t\epsilon^{2}L_{i})
, unless a
degenerate case
happens, in which
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{reduce}}_{i}=\emptyset
and

\omega(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}})=O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})(\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}}\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})+L_{i}).

(5)

For every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
,
\mathcal{X}
satisfies the three properties (
P1’
)-(
P3’
) with constant
g=31
.

The total node degree of
\mathcal{X}
in
\mathcal{G}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
in
Lemma

12.1
is worst than the total node degree of
\mathcal{X}
in
Lemma

11.1
by a factor of
1/\epsilon
. Furthermore, Item (4) of
Lemma

12.1
is qualitatively different from Item (4) of
Lemma

11.1
and we no longer can bound the size of
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
in the degenerate case. All of these are due to the fact that the stretch
t=1+\epsilon<2
when
\epsilon<1
.

Next we show to construct
H_{i}
given that we can construct a set of subgraphs
\mathbb{X}
as claimed in
Lemma

12.1
. The proof of
Lemma

12.1
is deferred to
Section

12.2
.

12.1
Constructing
H_{i}
: Proof of
Lemma

10.3
for
t=1+\epsilon
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}(\mathcal{X})=\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
for each
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}=\cup_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i})
be the set of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edges that are contained in subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}
. The construction of
H_{i}
is exactly the same as the construction of
H_{i}
in
Section

11.1
: first, add every edge of
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
to
H_{i}
, and then apply
\mathsf{SSO}
on the subgraph of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
induced by
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{reduce}}
. Furthermore,
7.10
and
11.3
hold here.

Recall that
F^{\sigma}_{i}
is the set of edges in
E^{\sigma}_{i}
that correspond to
\mathcal{E}_{i}
. By the same proof in
Lemma

11.2
we have:

Lemma 12.2
.

For every edge
(u,v)\in F^{\sigma}_{i}
,
d_{H_{<L_{i}}}(u,v)\leq t(1+\max\{s_{\mathsf{SSO}}(2g),6g\}\epsilon)w(u,v)
.

Next, we bound the total weight of
H_{i}
.

Lemma 12.3
.

w(H_{i})\leq\lambda\Delta_{i+1}+a_{i}
for
\lambda=O(\chi\epsilon^{-1}+\epsilon^{-2})
and
a_{i}=O(\chi\epsilon^{-1}+\epsilon^{-2})w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i})+O(L_{i}/\epsilon^{2})
.

Proof:
First, we consider the non-degenerate case. Note that edges in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{redunt}}_{i}
are not added to
H_{i}
. Let
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{+}=\cup_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})
and
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{-}=\cup_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}}\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})
.
Let
F^{(a)}_{i}
be the set of edges added to
H_{i}
in the construction in Step
a
,
a\in\{1,2\}
.

By the construction in Step 1,
F^{(1)}_{i}
includes edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
. Let
A^{(1)}\subseteq F^{(1)}_{i}
be the set of edges incident to at least one node in
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{+}
and
A^{(2)}=F^{(1)}_{i}\setminus A^{(1)}
. By Item (1) in
Lemma

12.1
, the total weight of the edges added to
H_{i}
in Step 1 is:

\begin{split}w(A^{(1)}_{i})&=\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|/\epsilon)L_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:averagePotential-t1E}}}}}{{=}}O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))\penalty\ .\end{split}

(61)

By definition
A^{(2)}
is the set of edges with both endpoints in subgraphs of
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{-}
. Consider the orientation of
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}
as in
Lemma

12.1
. Then, every edge of
A^{(2)}
is an out-going edge from some node in a graph in
\mathbb{X}^{-}
. For each graph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}
, by Item (4) of
Lemma

12.1
, the total weight of incoming edges of
\mathcal{X}
is
O(tL_{i})=O(1/\epsilon^{2})\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})
. Thus, we have:

\begin{split}w(A^{(2)}_{i})&=O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}}\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))\penalty\ .\end{split}

(62)

Thus, by
Equations

61
and
62
, we have
w(F^{(1)})=O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))
. By the exactly the same argument in
Lemma

12.3
, we have that
w(F^{(2)})=O(\chi/\epsilon)(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))
. This gives:

\begin{split}w(H_{i})&=O(\chi/\epsilon+1/\epsilon^{2})(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))\leq\lambda(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))\end{split}

(63)

for some
\lambda=O(\chi/\epsilon+1/\epsilon^{2})
.

It remains to consider the degenerate case, and in which case, we only add to
H_{i}
edges corresponding to
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}
. Thus, by Item (4) of
Lemma

12.1
, we have:

w(H_{i})=O(\frac{L_{i}}{\epsilon^{2}})\leq\lambda\cdot(\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}))+O(\frac{L_{i}}{\epsilon^{2}}),

(64)

since
\Delta_{i+1}+w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i})=\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}}\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})
by Item (1) in
11.3
. Thus, the lemma follows from
Equations

64
and
63
.

\Box

We are now ready to prove
Lemma

10.3
for the case
t=1+\epsilon
.

Proof:
[Proof of Item 2 of
Lemma

10.3
] The fact that subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}
satisfy the three properties (
P1’
)-(
P3’
) with constant
g=31
follows from Item (5) of
Lemma

12.1
. The stretch in
H_{<L_{i}}
of edges in
F^{\sigma}_{i}
follows from
Lemma

12.2
.

By
Lemma

12.3
,
w(H_{i})\leq\lambda\Delta_{i+1}+a_{i}
where
\lambda=O(\chi\epsilon^{-1}+\epsilon^{-2})
and
a_{i}=O(\chi\epsilon^{-1}+\epsilon^{-2})w(\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i})+O(L_{i}/\epsilon^{2})
. It remains to show that
A=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}a_{i}=O(\chi\epsilon^{-1}+\epsilon^{-2})
. Observe that

\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}O(\frac{L_{i}}{\epsilon^{2}})\penalty\ =\penalty\ O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})\sum_{i=1}^{i_{\max}}\frac{L_{i_{\max}}}{\epsilon^{i_{\max}-i}}\penalty\ =\penalty\ O(\frac{L_{i_{\max}}}{\epsilon^{2}(1-\epsilon)})\penalty\ =\penalty\ O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})w(\mathrm{MST})\penalty\ ;

here
i_{\max}
is the maximum level. The last equation is due to that
\epsilon\leq 1/2
and every edge has weight at most
w(\mathrm{MST})
since the weight of every is the shortest distance between its endpoints. By Item (2) of
11.3
,
\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}^{in}_{i}\leq w(\mathrm{MST})
. Thus,
A=O(\chi/\epsilon^{2})+O(1/\epsilon^{2})
as desired.

\Box

12.2
Clustering

In this section, we prove
Lemma

12.1
. The construction of
\mathbb{X}
has 5 steps. The first four steps are exactly the same as the first four steps in the construction in
Section

11
. In Step 5, we construct
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}_{5}
differently, taking into account of edges in
\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i}^{close}
in
Equation

56
. Recall that when the stretch parameter
t\geq 2
, we show that edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}
corresponding to
\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i}^{close}
are added to
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}}
(implicitly in
Lemma

11.27
). However, when
t=1+\epsilon
, we could not afford to do so, and the construction in Step 5 will take care of these edges.

Steps 1-4.

The construction of Steps 1 to 4 are exactly the same as Steps 1-4 in
Section

11.2
to obtain three sets of clusters
\mathbb{X}_{1},\mathbb{X}_{2}
and
\mathbb{X}_{4}
whose properties are described in
Lemmas

11.5
,
11.7
,
11.9
and
11.10
. After the four steps, we obtain the forest
\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}
, where every tree is a path. In particular, for every edge
(\bar{\mu},\bar{\nu})\in\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
with both endpoints in
\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}
, either (i) the edge is in
\bar{\mathcal{E}}^{close}_{i}(\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i})
, or (ii) at least one of the endpoints must belong to a low-diameter tree of
\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}
or (iii) in a (red) suffix of a long path in
\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}
of augmented diameter at most
L_{i}
.

Before moving on to Step 5, we need a preprocessing step in which we find all edges in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{redunt}}_{i}
. The construction of Step 5 relies on edges that are not in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{redunt}}_{i}
.

Constructing
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}}
and
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}-}
.

Let
\widetilde{F}^{(5)}_{i}
be obtained from
\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}
by uncontracting the contracted nodes. We apply the greedy algorithm. Initially, both
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}}
and
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}-}
are empty sets. We construct a graph
\mathcal{H}_{i}=(\mathcal{V}_{i},\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}\cup\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}-},\omega)
, which initially only include edges in
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
. We then consider every edge
\mathbf{e}=(\nu\cup\mu)\in\mathcal{E}_{i}
, where both endpoints are in
\mathcal{V}(\widetilde{F}^{(5)}_{i})
, in the non-decreasing order of the weight. If:

d_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}(\nu,\mu)\leq(1+6g\epsilon)\omega(\mathbf{e})\penalty\ ,

(65)

then we add
\mathbf{e}
to
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}}
. Otherwise, we add
\mathbf{e}
to
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}-}
(and hence to
\mathcal{H}_{i}
). Note that the distance in
\mathcal{H}_{i}
in
Equation

65
is the augmented distance. We have the following observation which follows directly from the greedy algorithm.

Observation 12.4
.

For every edge
\mathbf{e}=(\nu,\mu)\in\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}-}
,
d_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}(\nu,\mu)\geq(1+6g\epsilon)\omega(\mathbf{e})
.

Step 5.

Let
\overline{P}
be a path in
\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}
obtained by Item (5) of
Lemma

11.10
. We construct two sets of subgraphs, denoted by
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}_{5}
and
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{pref}}_{5}
, of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
. The construction is broken into two steps. Step 5A is only applicable when
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}\not=\emptyset
. In Step 5B, we need a more involved construction by
[
50
]
, as described in
Lemma

12.5
.

•

(Step 5A)
If
\overline{P}
has augmented diameter at most
6L_{i}
, let
\mathbf{e}
be an
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
edge connecting
\widetilde{P}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}
and a node in some subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}
;
\mathbf{e}
exists by
Lemma

11.10
. We add both
\mathbf{e}
and
\widetilde{P}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}
to
\mathcal{X}
.

•

(Step 5B)
Otherwise, the augmented diameter of
\overline{P}
is at least
6L_{i}
. Let
\{\overline{Q}_{1},\overline{Q}_{2}\}
be the suffix and prefix of
\overline{P}
such that
\overline{Q}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}_{1}
and
\overline{Q}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}_{2}
have augmented diameter at least
L_{i}
and at most
2L_{i}
. If
\overline{Q}_{j}
,
j\in\{1,2\}
is connected to a node in a subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}
via an edge
e\in\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
, we add
\tilde{Q}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}_{j}
and
e
to
\mathcal{X}
. If
\overline{Q}_{j}
contains an endpoint of
\overline{P}
, we add
\widetilde{Q}_{j}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}
to
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{pref}}_{5}
.

Next, denote by
\overline{P}^{{}^{\prime}}
the path obtained by removing
\overline{Q}_{1},\overline{Q}_{2}
from
\overline{P}
. We then apply the construction in
Lemma

12.5
to
\overline{P}^{{}^{\prime}}
to obtain a set of subgraphs
\mathbb{C}_{5}(\overline{P}^{{}^{\prime}})
and an orientation of edges in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}-}_{i}(\overline{P}^{\prime})
, the set edges of
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}-}_{i}
with both endpoints in the uncontraction of
\overline{P}^{{}^{\prime}}
. We add all edges of
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}-}_{i}(\overline{P}^{\prime})
to a set
\mathcal{E}^{(5B)}_{i}
(which is initially empty). We then add all subgraphs in
\mathbb{C}_{5}(\overline{P}^{{}^{\prime}})
to
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}_{5}
.

The construction of Step 5B is described in the following lemma, which is a slight adaption of Lemma 6.17 in
[
50
]
. See
Figure

11
for an illustration. The construction crucially exploit the fact that
d_{\mathcal{H}_{i}}(\nu,\mu)\leq(1+6g\epsilon)\omega(\mathbf{e})
.

Lemma 12.5

(Step 5B, Lemma 6.17 in
[
50
]
)
.

Let
\overline{P}
be a path in
\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}
.
Let
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}-}_{i}(\overline{P})
be the edges of
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}-}_{i}

with both endpoints in
\widetilde{P}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}
. We can construct a set of subgraphs
\mathbb{C}_{5}(\overline{P})
such that:

(1)

Subgraphs in
\mathbb{C}_{5}(\overline{P})
contain every node in
\widetilde{P}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}
.

(2)

For every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{C}_{4}(\overline{P})
,
\zeta L_{i}\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq 5L_{i}
. Furthermore,
\mathcal{X}
is a subtree of
\widetilde{P}^{\mathsf{uctrt}}
and some edges in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}-}_{i}(\overline{P})
whose both endpoints are in
\mathcal{X}
.

(3)

There is an orientation of edges in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}-}_{i}(\overline{P})

such that, for any subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{C}_{5}(\overline{P})
, if the total number of out-going edges incident to nodes in
\mathcal{X}
is
t
for any
t\geq 0
, then:

\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(t\epsilon^{2})L_{i}

(66)

Figure 11:
A path
\overline{P}
, a cluster
\mathcal{X}
, and a set of (blue) edges in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}-}_{i}(\overline{P})
.
White nodes are uncontracted nodes and black nodes are those in contracted nodes (triangular shapes).
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})
is proportional to the number of out-going edges from nodes in
\mathcal{X}
, which is 3 in this case; there could be edges with both endpoints in
\mathcal{X}
.

We observe the following from the construction.

Observation 12.6
.

For every edge
\mathbf{e}\in\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}-}
, either at least one endpoint of
\mathbf{e}
is in a subgraph in
\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
, or both endpoints of
\mathbf{e}
are in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{(5B)}
.

The following lemma is analogous to
Lemma

11.13
.

Lemma 12.7
.

Every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}\cup\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
satisfies:

(1)

\mathcal{X}
is a subtree of
\widetilde{\mathrm{MST}}_{i}
if
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
.

(2)

\zeta L_{i}\leq\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq 20L_{i}
.

(3)

|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|=\Omega(1/\epsilon)
.

Furthermore, if
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
, then
\mathcal{X}
the uncontraction of a prefix/suffix subpath
\overline{Q}
of a long path
\overline{P}
, and additionally, the (uncontraction of) other suffix
\overline{Q}^{\prime}
of
\overline{P}
is augmented to a subgraph in
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}
, unless
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}=\emptyset
.

In the next section, we prove
Lemma

12.1
.

12.2.1
Constructing
\mathbb{X}
and the partition of
\mathcal{E}_{i}
: Proof of
Lemma

12.1

We distinguish two cases:

Degenerate Case.

The degenerate case is the case where
\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}=\emptyset
. In this case, we set
\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{X}^{-}=\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}\cup\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
, and
\mathbb{X}^{+}=\emptyset
.

Non-degenerate case.

We define:

\begin{split}\mathbb{X}^{+}&=\mathbb{X}_{1}\cup\mathbb{X}_{2}\cup\mathbb{X}_{4}\cup\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}},\quad\mathbb{X}^{-}=\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}\\
\mathbb{X}&=\mathbb{X}^{+}\cup\mathbb{X}^{-}\end{split}

(67)

Next, we construct the partition of
\{\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i},\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{redunt}}_{i},\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{reduce}}\}
of
\mathcal{E}_{i}
. Recall that we constructed two edge sets
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}-}_{i}
above (
Equation

65
). We then construct
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
as described below. It follows that
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{reduce}}_{i}=\mathcal{E}_{i}\setminus(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}\cup\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}})
.

Constructing
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
:
Let
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{+}=\cup_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})
and
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{-}=\cup_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}}\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})
. First, we add all edges in
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}-}_{i}
to
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}
. Next, we add
(\cup_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}}\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\mathcal{E}_{i})
to
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
. Finally, for every edge
\mathbf{e}\in\mathcal{E}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}}
such that
\mathbf{e}
is incident to at least one node in
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{-}
, we add
\mathbf{e}
to
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
.

In the analysis below, we only explicitly distinguish the degenerate case from the non-degenerate case when it is necessary, i.e, in the proof Item (4) of
Lemma

12.1
. Otherwise, which case we are in is either implicit from the context, or does not matter.

We observe that Item (2) in
Lemma

12.1
follows directly from the construction of
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}}
. Henceforth, we focus on proving other items of
Lemma

12.1
. We first show Item (5).

Lemma 12.8
.

Let
\mathbb{X}
be the subgraph as defined in
Equation

67
. For every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
,
\mathcal{X}
satisfies the three properties (
P1’
)-(
P3’
) with
g=31
. Consequently, Item (5) of
Lemma

12.1
holds.

Proof:
We observe that property
(P1’)
follows directly from the construction. Property
(P2’)
follows directly from
Lemmas

11.5
,
11.7
,
11.10
and
12.7
. We now bound
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})
. The lower bound on
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})
follows directly from Item (3) of
Lemma

11.5
, Items (2) of
Lemmas

11.7
,
11.10
and
12.7
. For the upper bound, by the same argument in
Lemma

8.10
, if
\mathcal{X}
is initially formed in Steps 1-4, then
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq 31L_{i}
. Otherwise, by
Lemma

12.7
,
\mathsf{Adm}(\mathcal{X})\leq 5L_{i}
, which implies property
(P3’)
with
g=31
.

\Box

We observe that
Lemma

11.17
and
Lemma

11.18
holds for
\mathbb{X}^{+}
, which we restate below in
Lemma

12.9
and
Lemma

12.10
, respectively. In particular,
Lemma

12.10
implies Item (3) of
Lemma

12.1
.

Lemma 12.9
.

For any subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
such that
|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\geq\frac{2g}{\zeta\epsilon}
or
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(L_{i})
, then
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(\epsilon L_{i}|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
.

Lemma 12.10
.

\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})\geq 0
for every
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
and

\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})=\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{+}}\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon L_{i}).

We now prove Item (1) of
Lemma

12.1
, which we restate here for convenience.

Lemma 12.11
.

For every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
,
\deg_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}}(\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X}))=O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|/\epsilon)
where
\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}=(\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}})
, and
\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{X})\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{take}}
. Furthermore, if
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}
, there is no edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{reduce}}
incident to a node in
\mathcal{X}
.

Proof:
Let
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}^{+}}_{i}=\cup_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}}\mathcal{X}
. Note by the construction in Step 1 (
Lemma

11.5
), nodes in
\mathcal{V}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}^{+}}_{i}
have degree
O(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
. Let
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{(1)}
be the set of edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
with both endpoints in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{high}^{+}}_{i}
and
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}=\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{i}^{(1)}
. Also by the construction in Step 1 (
Lemma

11.5
), both endpoints of every edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}
have degree
O(1/\epsilon)
. Thus, for any
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
, the number of edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{(2)}
incident to nodes in
\mathcal{X}
is
O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|/\epsilon)
.

Next, we consider
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{(1)}
. Observe by the construction of
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
that there is no edge in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{(1)}
with two endpoints in two
different graphs
of
\mathbb{X}_{1}
. Furthermore, since
\mathcal{X}
is a tree for every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{1}
, the number of edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{(1)}
incident to nodes in
\mathcal{X}
is
O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|)
. This bound also holds for every subgraph
\mathcal{X}
not in
\mathbb{X}_{1}
since the number of incident edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{(1)}
is 0; this implies the claimed bound on
\deg_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{take}}_{i}}(\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X}))
.

For the last claim, we observe that nodes in subgraphs of
\mathbb{X}^{-}
are in
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{-}
. Thus, by the construction of
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
, every edge incident to a node of
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}
is either in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
or
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{redunt}}
.

\Box

We now focus on proving Item (4) of
Lemma

12.1
which we restate below.

Lemma 12.12
.

There exists an orientation of edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
such that for every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}
, if
\mathcal{X}
has
t
out-going edges for some
t\geq 0
, then
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|t\epsilon^{2}L_{i})
, unless a
degenerate case
happens, in which
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{reduce}}_{i}=\emptyset
and

\omega(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}})=O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})(\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}}\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})+L_{i}).

Proof:
First, we consider the non-degenerate case. Recall that
\{\mathcal{V}^{+}_{i},\mathcal{V}^{-}_{i}\}
is a partition of
\mathcal{V}_{i}
in the
construction of
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
. We orient edges of
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
as follows.

First, for any
\mathbf{e}=(\mu,\nu)\in\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
such that at least one endpoint, say
\mu\in\mathcal{V}_{i}^{+}
, we orient
\mathbf{e}
as out-going from
\mu
. (If both
\mu,\nu
are in
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{+}
, we orient
\mathbf{e}
arbitrarily). Remaining edges are subsets of
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{(5B)}
by
12.6
. We orient edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{(5B)}
as in the construction of Step 5B. For every subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{-}
, by construction, out-going edges incident to nodes in
\mathcal{X}
are in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{(5B)}
. By Item (3) of
Lemma

12.5
,
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=\Omega(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|t\epsilon^{2}L_{i})
.

It remains to consider the degenerate case. In this case, by the same argument in
Lemma

11.28
,
\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}=\overline{F}^{(4)}_{i}=\overline{F}^{(3)}_{i}
, and
\overline{F}^{(5)}_{i}
is a single (long) path. Furthermore,
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}=\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}-}
, and
|\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}|=2
. We orient edges in
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{(5B)}
as in the construction of Step 5B, and other edges of
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}
, which must be incident to nodes in subgraphs of
\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
, are oriented as out-going from subgraphs in
\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
. By Item (3) of
Lemma

12.1
, for any subgraph
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}_{5}
that has
t
out-going edges, the total weight of the out-going edges is at most
tL_{i}=O(1/\epsilon)\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})
. Thus,
\omega(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{(5B)})=O(1/\epsilon)\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{intrnl}}_{5}}\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})=O(1/\epsilon^{2})\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}}\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})
.

It remains to consider edges incident to at leas one node in a subgraph in
\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
by
12.6
. Let
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
. Observe that if
|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\geq\frac{2g}{\zeta\epsilon}
, then

\begin{split}\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})&=O(|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon L_{i})\qquad\mbox{(by \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lm:manynodes1E})}\\
&=O(|\deg_{\mathcal{G}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}}(\mathcal{X})|\epsilon^{2}L_{i})\qquad\mbox{(by Item (1) of \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{lm:ClusteringE})}\end{split}

Otherwise,
|\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{X})|\leq\frac{2g}{\zeta\epsilon}
, and hence
|\deg_{\mathcal{G}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}}(\mathcal{X})|=O(1/\epsilon^{2})
by Item (1) of
Lemma

12.1
. This implies that the total weight of edges incident to
\mathcal{X}
is at most
|\deg_{\mathcal{G}_{i}^{\mathsf{take}}}(\mathcal{X})|L_{i}=O(1/\epsilon^{2})(\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})+L_{i})
. Since
|\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}|=2
and
\Delta^{+}_{i+1}(\mathcal{X})\geq 0
for every
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}
by Item (3) of
Lemma

12.1
, we have that the total weight of edges ncident to at leas one node in a subgraph in
\mathbb{X}_{5}^{\mathsf{pref}}
is
O(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})(\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{X}}\Delta_{i+1}^{+}(\mathcal{X})+L_{i})
. The lemma now follows.

\Box
